刚果民主共和国金沙萨 疫苗接种 反 猴痘 协会

Facteurs associés à la faible vaccination contre la Mpox à Kinshasa en République Démocratique du Congo
作者： Maurice Felo Twendimbadi, Ange Mavula Ndeke, Horace Eketa Pene Akata, Dagobert N'tangu Menayame, Papy Kawata Dagumbu
摘要中文翻译引言:HIV–猴痘 共感染构成日益增长的挑战,因为感染人类免疫缺陷病毒(HIV)者的易感性增加,以及 传播 动力学的叠加。尽管在该人群中强烈推荐 疫苗接种,但疫苗接种覆盖率仍然低下,尤其在非洲。全球范围内,35,7% 的 HIV 感染者(PvVIH)已接种疫苗,但在非洲国家的比例明显更低,例如尼日利亚仅有 5% 接种了疫苗。这种低覆盖限制了对重症的预防,并凸显了改进疫苗获取策略的必要性。本研究的目标是确定在金沙萨影响 PvVIH 对 猴痘 的低疫苗接种率的因素。方法:这是一项在金沙萨市省 16 个卫生区的 64 家卫生机构开展的横断面分析研究。于 2025 年 10 月 5 日至 25 日期间,对 518 名 HIV 感染者进行数据收集。通过结构化、预先测试的问卷,由受过培训的调查员面对面访谈完成。统计分析使用 SPSS 25 版软件,显著性水平设为 5%。结果:结果显示,四分之三的 HIV 感染者(75,9%)年龄在 30 岁及以上。受访者中女性占多数(66,2%),30,1% 为家庭主妇。此外,43,6% 已婚,71,2% 受教育程度至少为高中。关于 PvVIH 对 猴痘 的认知,仅有 25,3% 曾听说过 猴痘。PvVIH 中接种 猴痘 的比例低,估计为 5,2%。导致低疫苗接种率的主要因素包括对 猴痘 症状认识有限(ORa = 4,8;IC95%: 1,72-13,80;p < 0,003),以及与 HIV 相关的污名化(ORa = 5,9;IC95%: 2,01-17,68;p < 0,001)。结论:本研究显示,金沙萨 HIV 感染者对 猴痘 的疫苗接种覆盖率极低,仅有 5,2% 的受试者接种疫苗,尽管其易感性增加。对 猴痘 的认知不足、持续的 HIV 污名和歧视是显著关联的因素。这些结果凸显了信息、预防和公平获取卫生服务方面的重大缺口,也表明迫切需要在 HIV 护理连续体中进一步整合 猴痘 疫苗接种,特别是针对免疫抑制患者。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在确定金沙萨艾滋病毒感染者(PLWH)中与 猴痘 低 疫苗接种 率相关的因素,强调艾滋病毒和 猴痘 共同感染所带来的挑战,特别是在非洲的 PLWH 中。
设计与对象
2025 年 10 月 5 日至 25 日期间,在金沙萨 16 个卫生区的 64 个医疗机构中开展了一项分析性横断面研究,涉及 518 名感染者。
核心方法
数据通过预先测试的结构化调查问卷收集,由经过培训的访谈员面对面进行,使用 SPSS 25 版进行统计分析,显着性水平为 5%。
主要结果
结果显示,75.9%的感染者年龄在30岁以上,其中66.2%为女性,30.1%为家庭主妇。此外,43.6% 的人已婚,71.2% 的人至少受过中等教育。只有25.3%的人听说过猴痘,疫苗接种的比例为5.2%。
解释与创新
与低 疫苗接种 率相关的主要因素是对 猴痘 症状的了解有限(ORa = 4.8;IC95%:1.72-13.80;p < 0.003)和 HIV 相关耻辱(ORa = 5.9;IC95%:2.01-17.68;p < 0.001)。
科研与公卫意义
该研究强调了信息、预防和公平获得卫生服务方面的巨大差距,强调迫切需要将 猴痘 疫苗接种 纳入艾滋病毒护理,特别是对于免疫功能低下的患者。
局限与证据强度
该研究的局限性包括横截面设计,它排除了因果推断,以及由于研究在金沙萨的背景而可能出现选择偏差。证据强度中等。

Facteurs associés à la faible vaccination contre la Mpox à Kinshasa en République Démocratique du Congo

Authors: Maurice Felo Twendimbadi, Ange Mavula Ndeke, Horace Eketa Pene Akata, Dagobert N'tangu Menayame, Papy Kawata Dagumbu
Original abstract (English)
Introduction: la co-infection VIH–Mpox constitue un défi croissant en raison de la vulnérabilité accrue des personnes vivant avec le virus de l'immunodéficience humaine (VIH) et de la superposition des dynamiques de transmission. Bien que la vaccination soit fortement recommandée dans ce groupe, la couverture vaccinale demeure faible, particulièrement en Afrique. Alors que 35,7% des Les personnes vivant avec le VIH (PvVIH) sont vaccinées au niveau mondial, les taux restent nettement inférieurs dans les pays africains, comme au Nigeria où seulement 5% ont reçu le vaccin. Cette faible couverture limite la prévention des formes graves et souligne la nécessité d'une meilleure stratégie d'accès au vaccin. L'objectif de l'étude était de déterminer les facteurs associés à la faible vaccination des PvVIH contre le Mpox à Kinshasa. Méthodes: il s'agissait d'une étude transversale analytique menée dans 64 établissements de soins de santé répartis dans 16 zones de santé de la ville-province de Kinshasa. Les données ont été recueillies auprès de 518 personnes vivant avec le VIH entre le 5 et le 25 octobre 2025. La collecte a été réalisée au moyen d'un questionnaire structuré et prétesté, administré lors d'entretiens en face à face par des enquêteurs formés. L'analyse statistique a été effectuée à l'aide du logiciel SPSS, version 25, en appliquant un seuil de signification de 5%. Résultats: les résultats montrent que trois personnes vivant avec le VIH sur quatre (75,9%) étaient âgées de 30 ans ou plus. La majorité des participantes étaient de sexe féminin (66,2%), et 30,1% exerçaient comme ménagères. Par ailleurs, 43,6% étaient mariées et 71,2% avaient atteint au moins le niveau d'études secondaires. Concernant les connaissances de Mpox chez PvVIH, seulement 25,3% avaient déjà entendu parler de la Mpox. La proportion de PvVIH vaccinées contre la Mpox était faible, estimée à 5,2%. Les principaux facteurs associés à la faible vaccination étaient connaissances limites des signes de la Mpox, (ORa = 4,8 ; IC95%: 1,72-13,80 ; p < 0,003) ; la stigmatisation liée au VIH (ORa = 5,9 ; IC95%: 2,01-17,68 ; p < 0,001). Conclusion: cette étude met en évidence une très faible couverture vaccinale contre la Mpox parmi les personnes vivant avec le VIH à Kinshasa, avec seulement 5,2% de sujets vaccinés malgré leur vulnéra-bilité accrue. Les faibles connaissances sur la Mpox, la persistance de la stigmatisation et de la discri-mination liées au VIH apparaissent comme des facteurs significativement associés à cette faible vaccina-tion. Ces résultats soulignent des lacunes importantes en matière d'information, de prévention et d'accès équitable aux services de santé. Ils indiquent également la nécessité urgente d'intégrer davantage la vac-cination Mpox dans le continuum de soins VIH, en particulier pour les patients immunodéprimés.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to determine the factors associated with low vaccination rates against mpox among people living with HIV (PLWH) in Kinshasa, highlighting the challenges posed by co-infection with HIV and mpox, particularly among PLWH in Africa.
Design and population
An analytical cross-sectional study was conducted in 64 healthcare facilities across 16 health zones in Kinshasa, involving 518 PLWH between October 5 and October 25, 2025.
Core methods
Data were collected through a pre-tested structured questionnaire administered face-to-face by trained interviewers using SPSS version 25 for statistical analysis with a significance level of 5%.
Main results
The results showed that 75.9% of PLWH were aged 30 years or older, with 66.2% being female and 30.1% working as homemakers. Additionally, 43.6% were married and 71.2% had at least a secondary education level. Only 25.3% had heard of mpox, and the vaccination rate was 5.2%.
Interpretation and novelty
The main factors associated with low vaccination rates were limited knowledge of mpox symptoms (ORa = 4.8; IC95%: 1.72-13.80; p < 0.003) and HIV-related stigma (ORa = 5.9; IC95%: 2.01-17.68; p < 0.001).
Scientific and public-health significance
The study highlights significant gaps in information, prevention, and equitable access to health services, emphasizing the urgent need to integrate mpox vaccination into HIV care, especially for immunocompromised patients.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study's limitations include the cross-sectional design, which precludes causal inference, and the potential for selection bias due to the study's setting in Kinshasa. The evidence strength is moderate.

全球免疫流行病学与猴痘 IIb系在MSM中的持续传播

Global immuno-epidemiology and the persistence of mpox clade IIb in MSM
作者： Gubela, N., Berghold, R., Bartel, A., Kühnert, D., von Kleist, M.
摘要中文翻译2022 年,猴痘 进化枝 IIb 在通过性接触发生男男性行为 (MSM) 的男性中在全球范围内传播。到 2022 年夏季,由于行为改变和免疫接种,欧洲和北美的病例数有所下降,2023 年几乎没有病例报告,随后是低水平的 传播。到 2022 年秋季,许多 传播 高风险易感个体可能已经接种疫苗。此外,由于携带 猴痘 的个体的传染性只有 2-4 周,因此维持 传播 链需要相当大的感染吞吐量。这就提出了一个问题:猴痘 是如何持续存在并转变为地方性循环的?我们在柏林 MSM 性接触网络上开发了基于代理的 猴痘 传播 模型,其中包含病毒脱落动力学、疫苗接种、感染源性免疫、免疫减弱和病例输入。校准模型后,我们估计,到 2023 年,当获得性免疫使 传播 网络支离破碎时,猴痘 在柏林灭绝的概率超过 80%。将分析扩展到欧洲 MSM 集合种群,将灭绝概率降低至 60% 以下,而纳入全球集合种群动态,则将灭绝概率降至接近零。我们发现,猴痘 的持续存在是通过流行病异步实现的:当 猴痘 在欧洲/美洲下降时,猴痘 传播 在亚洲继续存在,足够长的时间使免疫减弱部分恢复 传播 在欧洲的潜力,从而实现随后的重新输入和地方性循环。这些基于模型的发现得到了系统发育证据的支持,表明所有活跃的德国 传播 簇要么与(i)从美洲重新输入进化枝 F 相关,要么(ii)与亚洲相关的进化枝 C 和 E 的出现有关。我们对跨连接的全球 MSM 网络的异步 传播 的研究结果强调了全球免疫 流行病学 在实现 猴痘 持久性方面的作用,并强调了协调国际 监测 和控制工作的重要性。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
到 2022 年秋季,许多 传播 高风险易感个体可能已经接种疫苗。
设计与对象
提供的证据没有明确说明研究设计、设置或研究人群。
核心方法
我们在柏林 MSM 性接触网络上开发了基于代理的 猴痘 传播 模型,其中包含病毒脱落动力学、疫苗接种、感染源性免疫、免疫减弱和病例输入。
主要结果
到 2022 年夏季,由于行为改变和免疫接种,欧洲和北美的病例数有所下降,2023 年几乎没有病例报告,随后是低水平的 传播。
解释与创新
我们发现,猴痘 的持续存在是通过流行病异步实现的:当 猴痘 在欧洲/美洲下降时,猴痘 传播 在亚洲继续存在,足够长的时间使免疫减弱部分恢复 传播 在欧洲的潜力,从而实现随后的重新输入和地方性循环。
科研与公卫意义
我们对跨连接的全球 MSM 网络的异步 传播 的研究结果强调了全球免疫 流行病学 在实现 猴痘 持久性方面的作用,并强调了协调国际 监测 和控制工作的重要性。
局限与证据强度
这些基于模型的发现得到了系统发育证据的支持,表明所有活跃的德国 传播 簇要么与(i)从美洲重新输入进化枝 F 相关,要么(ii)与亚洲相关的进化枝 C 和 E 的出现有关。

Global immuno-epidemiology and the persistence of mpox clade IIb in MSM

Authors: Gubela, N., Berghold, R., Bartel, A., Kühnert, D., von Kleist, M.
Original abstract (English)
In 2022, mpox clade IIb spread globally in men who have sex with men (MSM) via sexual contact. By summer 2022, case numbers declined in Europe and Norther America due to behavior change and immunization, with almost no reported cases in 2023, followed by low-level transmission. By autumn 2022, many susceptible individuals at high risk of transmission may have become immunized. Moreover, because individuals with mpox are infectious for only 2-4 weeks, sustaining transmission chains requires considerable infection throughput. This raises the question: How did mpox persist and transition to endemic circulation? We developed an agent-based model of mpox transmission on the Berlin MSM sexual contact network that incorporates viral shedding kinetics, vaccination, infection-derived immunity, immune waning, and case importations. After calibrating the model, we estimated that the probability of mpox extinction in Berlin exceeded 80% in 2023, when acquired immunity fragmented the transmission network. Extending the analysis to a European MSM metapopulation reduced the extinction probability to less than 60%, whereas incorporating global metapopulation dynamics reduced it to nearly zero. We find that mpox persistence was enabled by epidemic asynchrony: When transmission declined in Europe/the Americas, mpox transmission continued in Asia, long enough for immune waning to partially restore transmission potential in Europe, thereby enabling subsequent re-importation and endemic circulation. These model-based findings are supported by phylogenetic evidence indicating that all active German transmission clusters are either linked to (i) re-importation of clade F from the Americas, or (ii) the emergence of clades C and E linked to Asia. Our findings on asynchronous transmission across connected global MSM networks highlight the role of global immuno-epidemiology in enabling mpox persistence and emphasize the importance of coordinated international surveillance and control efforts.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
By autumn 2022, many susceptible individuals at high risk of transmission may have become immunized.
Design and population
The supplied evidence does not clearly state the study design, setting, or study population.
Core methods
We developed an agent-based model of mpox transmission on the Berlin MSM sexual contact network that incorporates viral shedding kinetics, vaccination, infection-derived immunity, immune waning, and case importations.
Main results

Interpretation and novelty

Scientific and public-health significance

Limitations and evidence strength
These model-based findings are supported by phylogenetic evidence indicating that all active German transmission clusters are either linked to (i) re-importation of clade F from the Americas, or (ii) the emergence of clades C and E linked to Asia.

C 补充信息学术文献 · 研究C supplementaryAcademic literature · Research

从检测到预测:无症状排毒信息引导的质能平衡建模可预测机场传播的病毒输入

From detection to prediction: Asymptomatic shedding-informed mass-balance modeling predicts travel-borne viral importation at airports.
作者： Shi-Fu Peng, Yao Deng, Jia-Yi Liu, Xinyan Du, Liu Yang, Zhen Ding, Song-Zhe Fu
摘要中文翻译航空废水(AWS)作为一种非侵入性的旅行媒介病原体检测工具已崭露头角,但其应用仍主要停留在描述性层面,缺乏将环境浓度转化为定量感染动态的能力。本研究旨在通过将质能平衡逆向计算与混合捕获技术相结合,将AWS从被动病原体检测推进到主动预测框架。为解决质能平衡建模中的关键参数缺口——即无症状流感携带者的粪便排毒动力学,我们前瞻性地招募了238名非呼吸系统门诊患者,并通过RT-qPCR量化了粪便中流感A/B病毒载量(IAV/IBV)。无症状IAV和IBV排毒在所有年龄层中均被检测到,其中中位病毒载量分别为5.9和5.0 log10 copies/g,阳性率范围从41.2%(青少年)到62.1%(<10岁儿童),从而建立了首个包含携带者的质能平衡逆向计算经验性粪便排毒参数集。2025年1月至7月期间,我们前瞻性地从六个亚太航线收集了240份航空废水样本,并从中国南京的国际机场收集了60份纵向污水样本。通过RT-qPCR量化了诺如病毒、流感病毒、猴痘和登革病毒。建立了一个质能平衡逆向计算模型,将废水中的病毒浓度转化为估计的每周入境感染乘客数量。病毒基因组通过混合捕获面板进行富集,并进行高分辨率系统发育分析。预测框架估计了诺如病毒GII、流感A病毒和登革病毒的每周航空输入感染病例,揭示了与原发国流行趋势同步的区域输入曲线(95%一致性)。系统发育分析解析了多个独立的病毒引入事件。进一步采用易感-暴露-感染-恢复(SEIR)模型,我们预测了14天内的社区感染负担,并将预测结果与临床监测数据进行验证,显示出与IAV(r=0.71)和诺如病毒临床就诊(r=0.84)显著的一致性。通过将定量感染动态与谱系分辨率基因组学相结合,本研究确立了从定性病原体检测向预测暴发智能的范式转变。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
航空废水(AWS)作为一种非侵入性的旅行媒介病原体检测工具已崭露头角,但其应用仍主要停留在描述性层面,缺乏将环境浓度转化为定量感染动态的能力。
设计与对象
本研究旨在通过将质能平衡逆向计算与混合捕获技术相结合,将AWS从被动病原体检测推进到主动预测框架。
核心方法
建立了一个质能平衡逆向计算模型,将废水中的病毒浓度转化为估计的每周入境感染乘客数量。
主要结果
无症状IAV和IBV排毒在所有年龄层中均被检测到,其中中位病毒载量分别为5.9和5.0 log10 copies/g,阳性率范围从41.2%(青少年)到62.1%(<10岁儿童),从而建立了首个包含携带者的质能平衡逆向计算经验性粪便排毒参数集。
解释与创新
预测框架估计了诺如病毒GII、流感A病毒和登革病毒的每周航空输入感染病例,揭示了与原发国流行趋势同步的区域输入曲线(95%一致性)。
科研与公卫意义
通过将定量感染动态与谱系分辨率基因组学相结合,本研究确立了从定性病原体检测向预测暴发智能的范式转变。
局限与证据强度
进一步采用易感-暴露-感染-恢复(SEIR)模型,我们预测了14天内的社区感染负担,并将预测结果与临床监测数据进行验证,显示出与IAV(r=0.71)和诺如病毒临床就诊(r=0.84)显著的一致性。

From detection to prediction: Asymptomatic shedding-informed mass-balance modeling predicts travel-borne viral importation at airports.

Authors: Shi-Fu Peng, Yao Deng, Jia-Yi Liu, Xinyan Du, Liu Yang, Zhen Ding, Song-Zhe Fu
Original abstract (English)
Aircraft wastewater surveillance (AWS) has emerged as a non-invasive tool for travel-mediated pathogen detection, yet its utility remains largely descriptive without capacity to translate environmental concentrations into quantitative infection dynamics. This study aimed to advance AWS from passive pathogen detection to an active predictive framework by integrating mass balance back-calculation with hybrid-capture genomic surveillance. To resolve a critical parameter gap in mass-balance modeling-namely, the fecal shedding kinetics of asymptomatic influenza carriers, we prospectively enrolled 238 non-respiratory outpatients at Nanchang People's Hospital and quantified fecal influenza A/B virus loads (IAV /IBV) by RT-qPCR. Asymptomatic IAV and IBV shedding was detected across all age strata, with median viral loads of 5.9 and 5.0 log10 copies/g, respectively, and positivity rates ranging from 41.2% (adolescents) to 62.1% (children <10 years), thereby establishing the first empirical fecal shedding parameter set for carriage-inclusive mass-balance back-calculation. Between January and July 2025, we prospectively collected 240 aircraft wastewater samples from six Asia-Pacific flight routes and 60 longitudinal sewage samples from an international airport in Nanjing, China. RT-qPCR was conducted to quantify norovirus, influenza virus, mpox, and dengue virus. A mass balance back-calculation model was established to convert wastewater viral concentrations into estimated weekly numbers of infected inbound passengers. Viral genomes were enriched via hybrid-capture panels and subjected to high-resolution phylogenetic analysis. The predictive framework estimated weekly flight-imported infection cases for norovirus GII, influenza A virus and dengue virus, revealing distinct regional importation curves synchronized with epidemiological trends in countries of origin (95% concordance). Phylogenetic analysis resolved multiple independent viral introductions. Further employing a Susceptible-Exposed-Infectious-Recovered model, we forecasted 14-day community infection burden and validated predictions against clinical surveillance data, demonstrating significant concordance with IAV (r=0.71) and norovirus clinical visits (r=0.84). By bridging quantitative infection dynamics with lineage-resolution genomics, this study establishes a paradigm shift from qualitative pathogen detection to predictive outbreak intelligence.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Aircraft wastewater surveillance (AWS) has emerged as a non-invasive tool for travel-mediated pathogen detection, yet its utility remains largely descriptive without capacity to translate environmental concentrations into quantitative infection dynamics.
Design and population
This study aimed to advance AWS from passive pathogen detection to an active predictive framework by integrating mass balance back-calculation with hybrid-capture genomic surveillance.
Core methods
A mass balance back-calculation model was established to convert wastewater viral concentrations into estimated weekly numbers of infected inbound passengers.
Main results
Asymptomatic IAV and IBV shedding was detected across all age strata, with median viral loads of 5.9 and 5.0 log10 copies/g, respectively, and positivity rates ranging from 41.2% (adolescents) to 62.1% (children <10 years), thereby establishing the first empirical fecal shedding parameter set for carriage-inclusive mass-balance back-calculation.
Interpretation and novelty
The predictive framework estimated weekly flight-imported infection cases for norovirus GII, influenza A virus and dengue virus, revealing distinct regional importation curves synchronized with epidemiological trends in countries of origin (95% concordance).
Scientific and public-health significance
By bridging quantitative infection dynamics with lineage-resolution genomics, this study establishes a paradigm shift from qualitative pathogen detection to predictive outbreak intelligence.
Limitations and evidence strength
Further employing a Susceptible-Exposed-Infectious-Recovered model, we forecasted 14-day community infection burden and validated predictions against clinical surveillance data, demonstrating significant concordance with IAV (r=0.71) and norovirus clinical visits (r=0.84).

人类活动对新兴传染病的指纹影响。

The anthropogenic fingerprint on emerging infectious diseases.
作者： Rory Gibb, Sadie J. Ryan, David M. Pigott, Maria Del Pilar Fernandez, Renata L. Muylaert, Gregory F. Albery, Daniel J. Becker, Jason K. Blackburn, Hernan Caceres-Escobar, Michael Celone
摘要中文翻译摘要 新兴传染病是人类世的标志性现象1,2。人类驱动的生态系统变化以及人类与野生动物和家畜的接触可以推动人兽共患病和暴发媒介传染病的出现3–5,但这些过程如何塑造暴发风险景观仍知之甚少,除了少数被广泛研究的系统6–8。在此,我们整合了32种疾病共计58,319个暴发事件记录,系统地测试了16种假设的社会和环境驱动因素如何影响暴发的地理分布,同时考虑了检测和报告偏差。我们发现,暴发风险通常在混合景观中最高,即人类和家畜与森林及破碎生态系统共存的地区。这些综合因素,加上长期降水量的下降,对几种媒介媒介传染病(例如登革热、莱姆病和人兽共源性虫媒病毒)具有显著的共同影响。相比之下,直接传播的人兽共患病(例如埃博拉病毒和猴痘)共享的驱动因素较少,而大多数其他人类活动压力(例如森林砍伐、气候变暖和农业集约化)对不同疾病的影响差异较大。最一致的是,观察到的暴发地理分布受到医疗资源可及性的显著影响:每增加一小时前往医疗机构的旅行时间,报告数量就会中位数下降32%(不同疾病之间的范围为1.2–96.7%)。我们的研究结果强调,传染病的跨物种溢出和出现是多因素驱动的,没有一种适用于所有情况的策略能够有效预防大流行和疫情。基于生态系统的公共卫生干预措施9应始终基于特定系统的证据,并与对卫生系统和One Health病原体监测的更大投资相结合。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在了解人类驱动的变化如何塑造新兴传染病的风险,特别是关注影响猴痘病毒模式的因素。
设计与对象
本研究整合了32种疾病共计58,319个暴发事件记录,包括猴痘病毒,并系统地测试了16种假设的社会和环境驱动因素对暴发地理分布的影响。
核心方法
本研究考虑了检测和报告偏差,并分析了医疗资源可及性、生态系统变化以及人类与野生动物和家畜接触等因素如何影响暴发的地理分布。
主要结果
对于猴痘病毒,其暴发风险在混合景观中最高,即人类和家畜与森林及破碎生态系统共存的地区。医疗资源可及性与暴发报告之间存在最一致的关联,每增加一小时前往医疗机构的旅行时间,报告数量就会中位数下降32%。
解释与创新
研究结果强调,猴痘病毒的暴发受到多种因素的影响,没有单一策略能够有效防止其出现。此外,研究还表明,人类活动压力对不同疾病的影响存在差异。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果为针对猴痘病毒的公共卫生干预措施提供了信息,强调了需要基于特定系统的证据以及改善医疗资源可及性和病原体监测的重要性。
局限与证据强度
本研究受限于对暴发事件记录的依赖以及潜在的报告偏差,但由于其系统的方法和大规模数据集,证据是强有力的。

The anthropogenic fingerprint on emerging infectious diseases.

Authors: Rory Gibb, Sadie J. Ryan, David M. Pigott, Maria Del Pilar Fernandez, Renata L. Muylaert, Gregory F. Albery, Daniel J. Becker, Jason K. Blackburn, Hernan Caceres-Escobar, Michael Celone
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Emerging infectious diseases are a hallmark of the Anthropocene 1,2 . Human-driven ecosystem changes and human–wildlife–livestock contact can drive the emergence of zoonotic and vector-borne diseases 3–5 , but how much these processes shape landscapes of outbreak risk is poorly understood, beyond a few well-studied systems 6–8 . Here we consolidate 58,319 outbreak event records for 32 diseases and systematically test how 16 hypothesized social and environmental drivers impact outbreak geographies, while accounting for detection and reporting biases. We show that outbreak risks are typically highest in mosaic landscapes where people and livestock live alongside forests and fragmented ecosystems. These combined factors, along with long-term declines in precipitation, share strong impacts across several vector-borne diseases (for example, dengue, Lyme disease and zoonotic arboviruses). By contrast, directly transmitted zoonoses (for example, Ebola and mpox) share few common drivers, and the impacts of most other anthropogenic pressures (for example, deforestation, climate warming and agricultural intensification) vary widely between diseases. Most consistently, the observed geography of outbreaks is shaped by healthcare access: reporting declines by a median of 32% (range across diseases: 1.2–96.7%) for each additional hour’s travel time from a health facility. Our findings underscore that infectious disease spillover and emergence are multi-causal, and that no one-size-fits-all strategy can prevent epidemics and pandemics. Ecosystem-based public health interventions 9 should always follow system-specific evidence, and be paired with greater investment in health systems and One Health pathogen surveillance.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to understand how human-driven changes shape the risk of emerging infectious diseases, particularly focusing on the factors that influence outbreak patterns of mpox virus.
Design and population
The research consolidated 58,319 outbreak event records for 32 diseases, including mpox virus, and systematically tested the impact of 16 hypothesized social and environmental drivers on outbreak geographies.
Core methods
The study accounted for detection and reporting biases, and analyzed how factors such as healthcare access, ecosystem changes, and human–wildlife–livestock contact influence the geography of outbreaks for mpox virus.
Main results
Outbreak risks for mpox virus were found to be highest in mosaic landscapes where people and livestock coexist with forests and fragmented ecosystems. Healthcare access was most consistently linked to outbreak reporting, with a median decline of 32% for each additional hour’s travel time from a health facility.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings highlight that mpox virus outbreaks are influenced by multiple factors, and no single strategy can effectively prevent their emergence. The study also shows that the impacts of anthropogenic pressures vary between diseases.
Scientific and public-health significance
The results inform targeted public health interventions for mpox virus, emphasizing the need for system-specific evidence and improved healthcare access and pathogen surveillance.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on outbreak event records and potential reporting biases, but the evidence is strong due to its systematic approach and large dataset.

基于宿主信息的事件时间框架,连接流行病学延迟分布

A within-host-informed event-time framework linking epidemiological delay distributions
作者： Nyall Jamieson, Sang Woo Park, Kylie Ainslie, Jingsi Xu, Sebastian Funk, Christopher E. Overton, Lauren Ancel Meyers, Sam Abbott, Park, S. W., Ward, T.
摘要中文翻译了解病原体在人与人之间传播的时间和方式对于描述传播、解释监测数据以及设计有效的控制措施至关重要。然而,关键的流行病学延迟(包括潜伏期、潜伏期、代际间隔和连续间隔)难以估计,因为诸如感染、传染性、症状出现和传播等关键事件很少被直接观察到。当前的方法通常使用灵活的统计分布来估计这些延迟,而不是通过生成这些延迟的潜在生物过程显式地将它们联系起来,这使得理解其潜在生物学机制以及确保相关延迟估计之间的一致性变得困难。在这里,我们开发了一个基于动态的宿主内部病原体增长模型的随机事件时间框架,将流行病学延迟视为共享生物过程的耦合结果。通过将这些关键事件表示为相互关联的随机事件,该框架在推断参数时能够产生内部一致的延迟分布,并基于所假设的宿主模型(该模型控制病原体增长、清除、传染性和症状出现)进行机制性解释。该框架还通过统一的观察模型处理被审查和部分观察的感染和症状出现时间。在模拟研究中,该框架在不同样本量和疾病时间尺度下准确恢复了代际间隔特征。在应用于SARS-CoV-2和猴痘 传播配对数据时,该框架在多个分析中相对于传统对数正态延迟模型显示出改进的预测拟合度,尽管不同数据集和观察模型中改进的程度有所不同;它估计了Omicron的代际间隔比Delta更短,并揭示了与猴痘 传播数据兼容的替代宿主内部参数。这种机制性框架为独立拟合的延迟分布提供了可解释的生物学替代方案,产生了内部一致的流行病学延迟估计,并为整合生物学和流行病学数据奠定了基础,以更好地理解、预测和控制病原体传播。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
了解病原体在人与人之间传播的时间和方式对于描述传播、解释监测数据以及设计有效的控制措施至关重要。本研究旨在解决估计关键流行病学延迟(如潜伏期、潜伏期、代际间隔和连续间隔)的挑战,这些延迟由于诸如感染和症状出现等关键事件很少被直接观察而难以测量。
设计与对象
本研究开发了一个随机事件时间框架,将流行病学延迟与宿主内部病原体动态联系起来。它将该框架应用于SARS-CoV-2和猴痘 传播配对数据,分析模型在不同样本量和疾病时间尺度下的表现。
核心方法
该框架使用动态的宿主内部病原体增长模型,将流行病学延迟视为共享生物过程的结果。它结合了一个统一的观察模型,以处理被审查和部分观察的感染和症状出现时间,从而允许估计代际间隔和其他延迟参数。
主要结果
该框架在多个分析中相对于传统对数正态延迟模型显示出改进的预测拟合度,尽管不同数据集和观察模型中改进的程度有所不同。它估计了Omicron的代际间隔比Delta更短,并揭示了与猴痘 传播数据兼容的替代宿主内部参数。
解释与创新
该框架为独立拟合的延迟分布提供了可解释的生物学替代方案,产生了内部一致的流行病学延迟估计。它为整合生物学和流行病学数据奠定了基础,以更好地理解、预测和控制病原体传播。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现提供了一种方法,以提高流行病学延迟估计的准确性,从而为监测、诊断以及猴痘和其他病原体的控制策略提供信息。这种方法增强了对病原体传播动态建模的能力,并有助于设计针对性的干预措施。
局限与证据强度
本研究的结果基于模拟研究和对SARS-CoV-2和猴痘数据的应用,这可能限制其普遍适用性。该框架在不同数据集和观察模型中的表现表明其证据强度中等,因为预测拟合度的改进程度有所不同。

A within-host-informed event-time framework linking epidemiological delay distributions

Authors: Nyall Jamieson, Sang Woo Park, Kylie Ainslie, Jingsi Xu, Sebastian Funk, Christopher E. Overton, Lauren Ancel Meyers, Sam Abbott, Park, S. W., Ward, T.
Original abstract (English)
Understanding when and how pathogens spread from person to person is fundamental to characterizing transmission, interpreting surveillance data, and designing effective control measures. Yet key epidemiological delays -- including the latent period, incubation period, generation interval, and serial interval -- are difficult to estimate because key events such as infection, infectiousness, symptom onset, and transmission are rarely observed directly. Current approaches typically estimate these delays using flexible statistical distributions rather than explicitly linking them through the underlying processes that generate them, making it difficult to interpret the underlying biology and to ensure consistency across related delay estimates. Here, we develop a stochastic event-time framework built around a dynamic within-host pathogen-growth model that treats epidemiological delays as coupled outcomes of a shared biological process. By representing these key events as linked stochastic events, the framework produces internally consistent delay distributions while inferring parameters with mechanistic interpretations conditional on the assumed within-host model that govern pathogen growth, clearance, infectiousness, and symptom onset. The framework also accommodates censored and partially observed infection and symptom-onset times through a unified observation model. Across simulation studies, the framework accurately recovered generation-interval characteristics across sample sizes and disease time scales. In applications to SARS-CoV-2 and mpox transmission-pair data, the framework showed improved predictive fit relative to conventional lognormal delay models in several analyses, although the magnitude of improvement varied across datasets and observation models; it estimated shorter generation intervals for Omicron than Delta and revealed alternative within-host parameters compatible with the mpox transmission data. This mechanistic framework provides a biologically interpretable alternative to independently fitted delay distributions, yields internally consistent estimates of epidemiological delays, and establishes a foundation for integrating biological and epidemiological data to better understand, predict, and control pathogen transmission.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Understanding when and how pathogens spread from person to person is fundamental to characterizing transmission, interpreting surveillance data, and designing effective control measures. The study addresses the challenge of estimating key epidemiological delays, such as the latent period, incubation period, generation interval, and serial interval, which are difficult to measure directly due to the rarity of observing key events like infection and symptom onset.
Design and population
The study develops a stochastic event-time framework that links epidemiological delays to within-host pathogen dynamics. It applies this framework to SARS-CoV-2 and mpox transmission-pair data, analyzing how the model performs across different sample sizes and disease time scales.
Core methods
The framework uses a dynamic within-host pathogen-growth model to treat epidemiological delays as outcomes of a shared biological process. It incorporates a unified observation model to handle censored and partially observed infection and symptom-onset times, allowing for the estimation of generation intervals and other delay parameters.
Main results
The framework showed improved predictive fit relative to conventional lognormal delay models in several analyses, although the magnitude of improvement varied across datasets and observation models. It estimated shorter generation intervals for Omicron than Delta and revealed alternative within-host parameters compatible with the mpox transmission data.
Interpretation and novelty
The framework provides a biologically interpretable alternative to independently fitted delay distributions, yielding internally consistent estimates of epidemiological delays. It establishes a foundation for integrating biological and epidemiological data to better understand, predict, and control pathogen transmission.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings offer a method to improve the accuracy of epidemiological delay estimates, which can inform surveillance, diagnosis, and control strategies for mpox and other pathogens. This approach enhances the ability to model transmission dynamics and design targeted interventions.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study's results are based on simulation studies and applications to SARS-CoV-2 and mpox data, which may limit generalizability. The framework's performance across different datasets and observation models suggests moderate evidence strength, as improvements in predictive fit varied.

非流行区无症状猴痘病毒患病率的元分析,2022–2024

Meta-analysis of Asymptomatic Monkeypox Virus Prevalence in Nonendemic Regions, 2022–2024
作者： Kelsey J. MacCuish, Robert Snyder, Kelly A. Johnson, Edwin Chojolan, Awa Keinde, Kathleen Jacobson, Kayla Saadeh, Kelsey MacCuish, Robert E. Snyder, Kathleen R. Jacobson
摘要中文翻译本项元分析对2022年5月12日至2024年9月13日期间全球发表的与无症状猴痘病毒(MPXV)检测相关的文章进行了文献检索。主要结果是确定无症状MPXV检测的汇总患病率。我们采用随机效应模型进行分析,得出最终汇总的患病率为1.0%(95%预测区间为0.1%–11.2%)。结果在地理区域或研究设计上没有显著差异。尽管这些发现令人放心,但我们并未评估无症状个体的感染性或传播传播潜力。MPXV在全球范围内持续传播;其维持传播的病毒动态机制仍不明确,需要进一步研究。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
这项荟萃分析旨在确定 2022 年 5 月 12 日至 2024 年 9 月 13 日期间非流行地区无症状猴痘病毒 (MPXV) 检测的汇总 患病率。重点是 MPXV,特别是分支 IIb,它导致 猴痘(一种与天花相关的传染病)。
证据基础与方法
该综述包括对指定时间范围内全球发表的有关无症状 MPXV 检测的文章进行文献检索。进行随机效应分析以计算汇总的 患病率,为 1.0%(95% 预测区间为 0.1%–11.2%)。该分析排除了不符合无症状 MPXV 检测标准的研究。
共识与结论
发现无症状 MPXV 感染的汇总 患病率 为 1.0%(95% 预测区间为 0.1%–11.2%)。结果因地理区域或研究设计的不同而没有显着差异,表明非流行地区无症状 MPXV 患病率 的模式一致。
争议与缺口
无症状个体是否可以传播 MPXV 尚不确定。这部分是由于依赖需要存在病变的临床测试方法。此外,该研究没有评估无症状人群的传染性或 传播 潜力。
科研与实践启示
需要进一步研究来了解 MPXV 在人群中持续存在的病毒动力学。具体来说,研究应该调查无症状个体的 传播 潜力以及无症状感染在 MPXV 传播中的作用。

Meta-analysis of Asymptomatic Monkeypox Virus Prevalence in Nonendemic Regions, 2022–2024

Authors: Kelsey J. MacCuish, Robert Snyder, Kelly A. Johnson, Edwin Chojolan, Awa Keinde, Kathleen Jacobson, Kayla Saadeh, Kelsey MacCuish, Robert E. Snyder, Kathleen R. Jacobson
Original abstract (English)
In this meta-analysis, we performed a literature search for articles relating to asymptomatic monkeypox virus (MPXV) detections published worldwide during May 12, 2022–September 13, 2024. The primary outcome was to determine the pooled prevalence of asymptomatic MPXV detections. We conducted a random-effects analysis that yielded a final pooled prevalence of 1.0% (95% prediction interval 0.1%–11.2%). Results did not substantially differ by geographic region or study design. Although those findings are reassuring, we did not evaluate infectivity or transmission potential among asymptomatic persons. MPXV continues to circulate globally; the viral dynamics that enable its persistence remain poorly understood and merit further study.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This meta-analysis aimed to determine the pooled prevalence of asymptomatic monkeypox virus (MPXV) detections in nonendemic regions during May 12, 2022–September 13, 2024. The focus was on MPXV, specifically clade IIb, which causes mpox, an infectious disease related to smallpox.
Evidence base and method
The review included a literature search for articles on asymptomatic MPXV detections published globally during the specified timeframe. A random-effects analysis was conducted to calculate the pooled prevalence, which was 1.0% (95% prediction interval 0.1%–11.2%). The analysis excluded studies that did not meet the criteria for asymptomatic MPXV detection.
Consensus and conclusions
The pooled prevalence of asymptomatic MPXV infections was found to be 1.0% (95% prediction interval 0.1%–11.2%). Results did not vary significantly by geographic region or study design, indicating a consistent pattern of asymptomatic MPXV prevalence across nonendemic areas.
Controversies and gaps
There is uncertainty regarding whether asymptomatic individuals can transmit MPXV. This is partly due to the reliance on clinical testing methods that require the presence of lesions. Additionally, the study did not assess infectivity or transmission potential among asymptomatic persons.
Research and practice implications
Further research is needed to understand the viral dynamics that allow MPXV to persist in the population. Specifically, studies should investigate the transmission potential of asymptomatic individuals and the role of asymptomatic infections in the spread of MPXV.

A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

对新兴流行病时代 猴痘 准备、预测、预防和 监测 的人工智能应用的范围审查

A scoping review of artificial intelligence applications for mpox preparedness, prediction, prevention, and surveillance in the era of emerging epidemics
作者： Mohan Bilikallahalli Sannathimmappa, Vinod Nambiar, Shahenaz Satti
摘要中文翻译这项综述系统地梳理了人工智能在猴痘疫情连续体中的应用,揭示了诊断和预测工具方面的有前景进展,同时指出了数据质量、验证和公平性方面的关键差距,这些差距必须解决才能实现人工智能在现实世界中的有效应用。目标 系统地梳理人工智能在猴痘疫情连续体中的应用范围、方法、性能和转化成熟度,并识别研究差距以指导未来应对新兴疫情的准备。方法 我们按照PRISMA扩展综述框架(PRISMA-ScR)进行了综述。结果 深度学习图像分类器在诊断文献中占主导地位,据报道,其在区分猴痘皮肤病变与临床类似病例中的准确率和曲线下面积值范围约为0.83至0.99。1 研究的新颖性 一些早期综述已经探讨了人工智能在猴痘中的应用。因此,我们不声称是该领域的首个综述,也不声称具有时间上的优先性。相反,本综述的贡献在于方法学和结构上,其基于三个特征。文章摘要 人工智能生成的文本 由于猴痘的再次爆发,传统疾病应对和响应策略中的重大差距已被凸显,需要创新方法来增强公共卫生努力。摘要 背景 2022–2024年全球猴痘(以前称为猴痘)疫情的再次爆发,以及接连发布的国际关注公共卫生紧急情况声明,暴露出传统疾病应对、预测、预防和监测方面的持续薄弱环节。人工智能(AI)和机器学习(ML)提供了可扩展的工具以增强...
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本次范围界定审查旨在系统地绘制 猴痘 流行病连续体中人工智能 (AI) 应用的范围、方法、性能和转化成熟度,重点关注准备、预测、预防和 监测。该审查强调需要解决研究差距,以指导未来对新出现的流行病的准备。
证据基础与方法
范围界定审查遵循 PRISMA 范围界定审查扩展 (PRISMA-ScR) 框架。它检索了 PubMed、Scopus、Web of Science、IEEE Xplore 和 Embase,从数据库建立到 2025 年 9 月 30 日。符合条件的记录集中在 2018 年至 2025 年之间。经过单个审稿人的重复数据删除和两阶段筛选后,在四个主题领域下绘制和综合了符合预定义资格标准的 48 项研究:诊断 和检测、预测和预测、 监测 和数字 流行病学,以及药物和 疫苗 发现。
共识与结论
深度学习图像分类器在诊断文献中占主导地位,其报道的区分 猴痘 皮肤病变与临床模拟的准确度和曲线下面积值范围约为 0.83 至 0.99。时间序列和神经网络模型(ARIMA、LSTM、GRU 和集成亚流行框架)产生了可靠的短期病例预测,而自然语言处理管道则近乎实时地跟踪公众情绪和错误信息。人工智能辅助的 基因组监测、废水监测以及基于结构的药物和表位发现代表了快速新兴但不太成熟的领域。
争议与缺口
报告的性能指标是作者报告的在异构数据集和验证协议下获得的值,并且没有合并。在所有研究中,纳入的初步研究经常报告数据集小且不平衡、外部验证有限以及临床整合薄弱。诊断成像、流行病学预测、基因组分析、数字信息流行病学和治疗发现方面的证据仍然支离破碎。
科研与实践启示
前瞻性验证、公平的数据治理、可解释性以及融入公共卫生工作流程是未来流行病对现实世界产生影响的先决条件。人工智能辅助的 基因组监测、废水监测以及基于结构的药物和表位发现需要进一步开发和验证,以确保其在现实环境中的有效性和适用性。

A scoping review of artificial intelligence applications for mpox preparedness, prediction, prevention, and surveillance in the era of emerging epidemics

Authors: Mohan Bilikallahalli Sannathimmappa, Vinod Nambiar, Shahenaz Satti
Original abstract (English)
This scoping review systematically maps the applications of artificial intelligence across the mpox epidemic continuum, revealing promising advancements in diagnostic and predictive tools while identifying critical gaps in data quality, validation, and equity that must be addressed for effective real-world implementation. Objective To systematically map the scope, methods, performance, and translational maturity of AI applications across the mpox epidemic continuum, and to identify research gaps that should guide future preparedness for emerging epidemics. Methods We conducted a scoping review following the PRISMA Extension for Scoping Reviews (PRISMA-ScR) framework. Results Deep-learning image classifiers dominated the diagnostic literature, with reported accuracies and area-under-the-curve values ranging from approximately 0.83 to 0.99 for distinguishing mpox skin lesions from clinical mimics. 1 Novelty of the study Several earlier reviews have already examined AI for mpox. We therefore do not claim to be the first to review this field, and we make no claim of chronological primacy. Instead, the contribution of this scoping review is methodological and structural, and rests on three features. Article summary AI generated The resurgence of mpox has highlighted significant gaps in traditional disease preparedness and response strategies, necessitating innovative approaches to enhance public health efforts. Abstract Background The 2022–2024 global resurgence of mpox (formerly monkeypox) and the successive declarations of a Public Health Emergency of International Concern have exposed persistent weaknesses in conventional disease preparedness, prediction, prevention, and surveillance. Artificial intelligence (AI) and machine learning (ML) offer scalable tools to strength...
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This scoping review aims to systematically map the scope, methods, performance, and translational maturity of artificial intelligence (AI) applications across the mpox epidemic continuum, with a focus on preparedness, prediction, prevention, and surveillance. The review highlights the need to address research gaps that should guide future preparedness for emerging epidemics.
Evidence base and method
The scoping review followed the PRISMA Extension for Scoping Reviews (PRISMA-ScR) framework. It searched PubMed, Scopus, Web of Science, IEEE Xplore, and Embase from database inception to 30 September 2025. Eligible records were concentrated between 2018 and 2025. After de-duplication and two-stage screening by a single reviewer, 48 studies meeting predefined eligibility criteria were charted and synthesized under four thematic domains: diagnosis and detection, prediction and forecasting, surveillance and digital epidemiology, and drug and vaccine discovery.
Consensus and conclusions
Deep-learning image classifiers dominated the diagnostic literature, with reported accuracies and area-under-the-curve values ranging from approximately 0.83 to 0.99 for distinguishing mpox skin lesions from clinical mimics. Time-series and neural-network models (ARIMA, LSTM, GRU, and ensemble sub-epidemic frameworks) produced credible short-term case forecasts, while natural-language-processing pipelines tracked public sentiment and misinformation in near real time. AI-assisted genomic surveillance, wastewater monitoring, and structure-based drug and epitope discovery represented rapidly emerging but less mature domains.
Controversies and gaps
Reported performance metrics were author-reported values obtained under heterogeneous datasets and validation protocols, and were not pooled. Across studies, small and imbalanced datasets, limited external validation, and weak clinical integration were recurrently reported by the included primary studies. The evidence remains fragmented across diagnostic imaging, epidemiological forecasting, genomic analytics, digital infodemiology, and therapeutic discovery.
Research and practice implications
Prospective validation, equitable data governance, explainability, and integration into public-health workflows are prerequisites for real-world impact in future epidemics. AI-assisted genomic surveillance, wastewater monitoring, and structure-based drug and epitope discovery require further development and validation to ensure their effectiveness and applicability in real-world settings.

学生对猴痘的知识、态度和实践的认知行为方面:系统回顾

COGNITIVE-BEHAVIORAL ASPECTS OF STUDENTS’ KNOWLEDGE, ATTITUDES, AND PRACTICES TOWARD MONKEYPOX: A SYSTEMATIC REVIEW
作者： Dimas Setyadi Putra, Bachren Azra Saputra, Salvinia Salvy Prihanta, Sultoni Bagas Alfikri
摘要中文翻译背景:2022年1月至2024年7月,猴痘(猴痘)已感染103,048人,并导致229人死亡。约83.6%的病例与性活动有关,凸显了行为因素在疾病传播中的重要作用。学生的知识、态度和实践 (KAP) 不足可能会导致错误信息和次优的预防行为,特别是对于成为未来医疗保健专业人员的健康相关学科的学生。目的:本系统评价旨在确定学生中 猴痘 与 KAP 相关的水平和因素。方法:使用预定义的关键字和布尔运算符,通过 ScienceDirect、Scopus 和 PubMed 进行截至 2024 年 9 月的全面文献检索。根据预先定义的纳入和排除标准筛选符合条件的文章。使用乔安娜·布里格斯研究所 (JBI) 严格评估清单评估纳入研究的质量。结果:在三个数据库中确定的 44 篇文章中,有 6 项研究符合纳入标准。六项研究中的三项报告了良好的知识,五分之三的报告报告了积极的态度,两项研究中的一项报告了良好的做法。教育、信息相关和人口因素与 KAP 相关。结论:学生对 猴痘 的 KAP 因环境而异,知识范围从差到好,态度普遍良好,预防措施的证据有限。这些发现支持将新出现的传染病纳入相关课程,并改善学生获取可靠健康信息的机会。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本系统评价旨在确定学生中与 猴痘 相关的知识、态度和实践 (KAP) 相关的水平和因素。本综述重点关注 猴痘病毒、猴痘病毒和相关的 正痘病毒。
证据基础与方法
使用预定义的关键字和布尔运算符,通过 ScienceDirect、Scopus 和 PubMed 进行截至 2024 年 9 月的全面文献检索。根据预先确定的纳入和排除标准筛选符合条件的文章,并使用乔安娜·布里格斯研究所 (JBI) 关键评估清单评估纳入研究的质量。
共识与结论
学生对 猴痘 的 KAP 因环境而异,知识范围从差到好,态度普遍良好,但预防措施的证据有限。这些发现支持将新出现的传染病纳入相关课程,并改善学生获取可靠健康信息的机会。
争议与缺口
审查发现研究的质量和一致性存在异质性,只有六项研究符合纳入标准。关于学生预防措施的证据有限,而且研究也没有一致地报告人口因素。
科研与实践启示
这些发现表明,教育干预措施应纳入课程中,以提高学生对 猴痘 的知识和态度。还建议改善对可靠健康信息的获取,以支持预防措施。

COGNITIVE-BEHAVIORAL ASPECTS OF STUDENTS’ KNOWLEDGE, ATTITUDES, AND PRACTICES TOWARD MONKEYPOX: A SYSTEMATIC REVIEW

Authors: Dimas Setyadi Putra, Bachren Azra Saputra, Salvinia Salvy Prihanta, Sultoni Bagas Alfikri
Original abstract (English)
Background: Monkeypox (mpox) has infected 103,048 people and caused 229 deaths from January 2022 to July 2024. Approximately 83.6% of cases were associated with sexual activity, highlighting the important role of behavioral factors in disease transmission. Inadequate knowledge, attitudes, and practices (KAP) among students may contribute to misinformation and suboptimal preventive behaviors, particularly among students in health-related disciplines who become future healthcare professionals. Purpose: This systematic review aimed to identify the levels and factors associated with KAP regarding mpox among students. Methods: A comprehensive literature search through ScienceDirect, Scopus, and PubMed up to September 2024 using predefined keywords and Boolean operators. Eligible articles were screened according to predefined inclusion and exclusion criteria. The quality of the included studies was assessed using the Joanna Briggs Institute (JBI) critical appraisal checklist. Results: Of 44 articles identified across three databases, six studies met the inclusion criteria. Three of six studies reported good knowledge, three of five reported positive attitudes, and one of two studies reported good practices. Educational, information-related, and demographic factors were associated with KAP. Conclusion: Students’ KAP toward mpox varied across settings, with knowledge ranging from poor to good, generally favorable attitudes, and limited evidence on preventive practices. These findings support the integration of emerging infectious diseases into relevant curricula and improved access to reliable health information among students.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This systematic review aimed to identify the levels and factors associated with knowledge, attitudes, and practices (KAP) regarding mpox among students. The review focuses on mpox virus, monkeypox virus, and related orthopoxviruses.
Evidence base and method
A comprehensive literature search through ScienceDirect, Scopus, and PubMed up to September 2024 using predefined keywords and Boolean operators. Eligible articles were screened according to predefined inclusion and exclusion criteria, and the quality of the included studies was assessed using the Joanna Briggs Institute (JBI) critical appraisal checklist.
Consensus and conclusions
Students’ KAP toward mpox varied across settings, with knowledge ranging from poor to good, generally favorable attitudes, and limited evidence on preventive practices. These findings support the integration of emerging infectious diseases into relevant curricula and improved access to reliable health information among students.
Controversies and gaps
The review identified heterogeneity in the quality and consistency of the studies, with only six studies meeting the inclusion criteria. There is limited evidence on preventive practices among students, and the studies did not consistently report on demographic factors.
Research and practice implications
These findings suggest that educational interventions should be integrated into curricula to improve students’ knowledge and attitudes toward mpox. Improved access to reliable health information is also recommended to support preventive practices.

以一价买二:多诺万病和软下疳是否应该成为根除的目标?

Two for the price of one: should donovanosis and chancroid be targeted for eradication?
作者： Jacques Pépin, Philippe Mayaud, John Richens, David C. W. Mabey
摘要中文翻译天花被消灭半个世纪后,这一壮举尚未重演,尽管麦地那龙线虫仍有希望。本文探讨了是否应该根除两种罕见的生殖器溃疡病:多诺万病和软下疳。许多人认为,随着更有效的 治疗、将性传播感染管理纳入初级医疗保健、性工作者加强安全套使用以及推广男性包皮环切术,它们的下降可能会导致自发根除。然而,梅毒和 猴痘 的例子表明自发根除可能是一个谬论。多诺万病在一些国家零星发生,尤其是印度。在其他以前流行的国家(例如巴布亚新几内亚和印度尼西亚巴布亚地区),目前的流行病学情况尚不清楚。全球范围内的 发病率 每年可能有几百个案例,也许有一千个。有效干预措施的可用性、相对容易的临床 诊断、充分的实验室测试以及动物 诊断 的缺乏表明,可以通过增强的 监测 和更强大的接触者追踪系统来实现根除,该系统假定所有性接触者(包括性工作者)为 治疗。目前软下疳每年的病例数尚不清楚,但全球范围内可能有数十万例。在根除活动之前,第一阶段应通过对哨点诊所出现生殖器溃疡的患者进行分子检测,记录其在所有先前流行国家的状况。还应开展更多研究来调查皮肤溃疡中的杜克雷嗜血杆菌是否会引起生殖器感染。软下疳与性工作密切相关:这是必须关闭的水龙头。在该病仍然流行的情况下,应支持性工作者的服务,以治疗有症状的病例,加强安全套的使用,并定期使用阿奇霉素进行推定 治疗。鉴于其重叠的地理分布以及性工作者在 传播 动态中的主导作用,根除计划可以使用相同的人员同时解决这两种疾病。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述探讨了是否应该根除多诺万病和软下疳这两种罕见的生殖器溃疡性疾病。它考虑了它们的流行病学状况、自发根除的潜力以及根除计划的可行性。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对多诺万病和软下疳当前流行病学和临床状况的叙述性分析。它不报告特定的数据库、搜索日期或荟萃分析方法。作者重点关注 监测 和干预的一般观察和建议。
共识与结论
该审查表明,多诺万病和软下疳可能正在减少,并且有可能通过加强 监测、接触者追踪和性接触的 治疗 来根除。然而,它也指出,自发根除可能并不可靠,正如梅毒和 猴痘 所见。
争议与缺口
该审查强调了以前软下疳流行国家当前流行病学状况的不确定性。它还对杜克雷嗜血杆菌在引起生殖器感染中的作用以及减少 传播 的干预措施的有效性提出了疑问。
科研与实践启示
审查建议第一阶段使用分子检测记录所有以前流行的国家多诺万病和软下疳的状况。它还建议支持性工作者的服务,以治疗病例,加强安全套的使用,并定期使用阿奇霉素进行推定 治疗。

Two for the price of one: should donovanosis and chancroid be targeted for eradication?

Authors: Jacques Pépin, Philippe Mayaud, John Richens, David C. W. Mabey
Original abstract (English)
Half a century after smallpox eradication, this feat has not been repeated, although there is hope for the Guinea worm. This article examines whether two uncommon genital ulcerative diseases, donovanosis and chancroid, should be targeted for eradication. Many think that their decline has been such that they might be heading towards spontaneous eradication, following more effective treatments, integration of sexually transmitted infection management in primary healthcare, enhanced condom use by sex workers and promotion of male circumcision. However, examples of syphilis and mpox suggest spontaneous eradication may be a fallacy. Donovanosis occurs sporadically in several countries, especially India. In other formerly endemic nations (eg, Papua New Guinea and the Papua region of Indonesia), the current epidemiological situation is unclear. Worldwide incidence might be a few hundred cases annually, perhaps a thousand. The availability of effective interventions, relatively easy clinical diagnosis, adequate laboratory tests and the absence of animal reservoirs suggest that eradication could be achieved through enhanced surveillance and a stronger contact tracing system with presumptive treatment of all sexual contacts, including sex workers. Chancroid’s current annual caseload is unknown but likely in the hundreds of thousands of cases globally. Prior to eradication activities, a first phase should document its status in all previously endemic countries using molecular tests on patients presenting with genital ulcers in sentinel clinics. Additional research should investigate whether Haemophilus ducreyi in skin ulcers can cause genital infections. Chancroid is strongly associated with sex work: this is the tap that must be turned off. Where it remains endemic, sex workers’ services should be supported to treat symptomatic cases, enhance condom use and deliver periodic presumptive treatment with azithromycin. Given their overlapping geographic distribution and the preponderant role of sex workers in transmission dynamics, an eradication programme could address both diseases simultaneously, with the same personnel.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review explores whether donovanosis and chancroid, two uncommon genital ulcerative diseases, should be targeted for eradication. It considers their epidemiological status, potential for spontaneous eradication, and the feasibility of eradication programs.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative analysis of the current epidemiological and clinical status of donovanosis and chancroid. It does not report specific databases, search dates, or meta-analysis methods. The authors focus on general observations and recommendations for surveillance and intervention.
Consensus and conclusions
The review suggests that donovanosis and chancroid may be declining and could potentially be eradicated through enhanced surveillance, contact tracing, and treatment of sexual contacts. However, it also notes that spontaneous eradication may not be reliable, as seen with syphilis and mpox.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights uncertainty about the current epidemiological status of chancroid in formerly endemic countries. It also raises questions about the role of Haemophilus ducreyi in causing genital infections and the effectiveness of interventions in reducing transmission.
Research and practice implications
The review recommends a first phase of documenting the status of donovanosis and chancroid in all previously endemic countries using molecular tests. It also suggests supporting sex workers' services to treat cases, enhance condom use, and deliver periodic presumptive treatment with azithromycin.

具有大流行潜力病毒的废水监测公共卫生价值:一项建模研究

The public health value of wastewater surveillance for viruses with pandemic potential: a modelling study
作者： Dighe, A., Grassly, N. C., Whittaker, C.
摘要中文翻译新兴病毒的动物源性传播和早期人传人传播往往被临床检测所忽视。废水监测(WS)提供了低成本的互补病原体检测手段,但其对新兴病毒的价值仍不确定。我们回顾了人类排泄物中病毒排泄的证据,并将排泄数据、检测模型和模拟的传播动力学整合到一个定量框架中,以确定废水监测对哪些类型的新兴病毒能够提供最大价值。模拟显示,对于排泄量超过SARS-CoV-2 1/100 至 10 倍的病毒,废水监测可提高检测概率或速度,具体取决于临床症状出现的概率和传播模式。在感染时间集中导致每日排泄者数量激增的传播情景中,废水监测的价值最高。SARS-CoV-2、正痘病毒、流感A型病毒、拉沙病毒和扎伊尔埃博拉病毒的特征似乎比其他病毒更有利于废水监测。我们预计这一框架可以支持对新兴威胁进行更具针对性和基于证据的废水监测应用。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本研究聚焦于具有大流行潜力病毒的废水监测(WS)的公共卫生价值,特别是针对该病毒及其亚型。核心问题是废水监测是否能够提高对新兴病毒的检测概率或速度,尤其是那些与正痘病毒特征相似的病毒。
证据基础与方法
该综述是一项建模研究,整合了人类排泄物中病毒排泄的证据、检测模型以及模拟的传播动力学。作者将排泄数据与这些模型结合,构建了一个用于评估废水监测对新兴病毒实用性的定量框架。
共识与结论
模拟结果表明,废水监测能够提高对排泄量超过SARS-CoV-2的病毒的检测概率或速度,具体取决于临床症状出现的可能性和传播模式。在感染时间集中导致每日排泄者数量激增的传播情景中,废水监测的价值最高。
争议与缺口
本研究承认,废水监测对新兴病毒的价值仍存在不确定性,尤其是那些排泄量不高或临床症状出现概率较低的病毒。对于正痘病毒而言,缺乏直接的证据证明废水监测的有效性,因为研究结果是基于模拟和与其他病毒的比较得出的。
科研与实践启示
本研究开发的框架可以指导对新兴威胁进行更具针对性和基于证据的废水监测应用。它建议应优先考虑那些具有高排泄潜力和时间集中传播模式的病毒,例如与正痘病毒特征相似的病毒。

The public health value of wastewater surveillance for viruses with pandemic potential: a modelling study

Authors: Dighe, A., Grassly, N. C., Whittaker, C.
Original abstract (English)
Zoonotic spillover and early human-to-human transmission of emerging viruses are often missed by clinical surveillance. Wastewater surveillance (WS) offers low-cost complementary pathogen detection, but its value for emerging viruses remains uncertain. We reviewed evidence of viral shedding in human waste and combined shedding data, detection models and simulated transmission dynamics into a quantitative framework to identify for which types of emerging viruses WS could add most value. Simulations showed that WS improved probability or speed of outbreak detection for viruses shedding >1/100 to >10 times as much as SARS-CoV-2, depending on probability of clinical symptoms and diagnosis. Value added was highest for transmission scenarios with temporally concentrated infections resulting in spikes in daily shedders. Characteristics of SARS-CoV-2, Mpox, Influenza A, Lassa, and Zaire Ebola viruses appear more favourable for WS than others e.g. We anticipate this framework can support more targeted, evidence-based application of WS to emerging threats.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study focuses on the public health value of wastewater surveillance (WS) for viruses with pandemic potential, specifically examining the Mpox virus and its clades. The central question is whether WS can improve the probability or speed of outbreak detection for emerging viruses, particularly those with characteristics similar to Mpox.
Evidence base and method
The review is a modelling study that integrates evidence of viral shedding in human waste, detection models, and simulated transmission dynamics. The authors combined shedding data with these models to create a quantitative framework for assessing the utility of WS for emerging viruses.
Consensus and conclusions
The simulations demonstrated that wastewater surveillance improves the probability or speed of outbreak detection for viruses that shed more than SARS-CoV-2, depending on the likelihood of clinical symptoms and diagnosis. The value of WS is highest in scenarios with temporally concentrated infections leading to spikes in daily shedders.
Controversies and gaps
The study acknowledges that the value of WS for emerging viruses remains uncertain, particularly for those that do not exhibit high shedding rates or have low clinical symptom probabilities. There is a lack of direct evidence on the effectiveness of WS for Mpox specifically, as the findings are based on simulations and comparisons with other viruses.
Research and practice implications
The framework developed in this study can guide more targeted and evidence-based use of wastewater surveillance for emerging threats. It suggests that WS should be prioritized for viruses with high shedding potential and temporally concentrated transmission patterns, such as those similar to Mpox.

全球猴痘:当前情况 - cdc.gov

Global Monkeypox: Current Situation - cdc.gov
新闻简报美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)于2026年9月25日发布的报告概述了持续的全球猴痘暴发,该疫情始于2022年,已导致122个国家出现超过100,000例病例,其中包括此前未报告的115个国家。该暴发主要由IIb分支猴痘病毒驱动,其死亡率(死亡率)低于0.1%。在美国,自2024年11月以来,至少20个州已报告超过60例实验室确认的I分支猴痘病例,且越来越多的病例与国内传播相关。这些病例主要发生在同性性行为的男性群体中,许多病例与前往中非和东非地区旅行或与旅行者发生性接触有关。CDC强调,对于大多数美国人群而言,I分支猴痘的威胁仍然较低,而II分支猴痘仍在持续传播。该机构正在积极监测病例,提供疫苗接种建议,并与公共卫生部门及国际合作伙伴合作,以遏制暴发。CDC还通过现有的监测系统增强早期检测能力,包括污水检测,并向相关地区提供技术支持。报告强调了疫苗接种作为防止猴痘传播的关键工具,特别是对高风险人群或前往受影响地区的人士而言。

时间
The current situation report was published on 2026-09-25. The outbreak began in 2022, and recent outbreaks have spread through intimate or sexual contact. Since August 2026, domestic transmission has increased in the U.S. (N10, N14, N23, N25, N26, N48, N52).
地点与对象
该报告涵盖全球猴痘暴发,重点聚焦于美国。在美国,至少20个州已报告病例,且大多数病例发生在同性性行为的男性群体中。病例还出现在中非和东非、西欧及其他地区(N10,N19,N20,N21,N24,N35,N43,N48)。
事件
该报告详细描述了持续的全球猴痘暴发,该疫情始于2022年,并强调了IIb分支猴痘的传播。同时,报告指出Ib分支猴痘在美国已转变为持续的国内传播,并指出Ib分支病例的死亡率较低(N17,N24,N28,N36,N38,N43)。
规模、影响与风险
持续的IIb分支猴痘暴发已导致全球122个国家出现超过100,000例病例,其中包括此前未报告的115个国家。在美国,自2024年11月以来,已报告超过60例实验室确认的I分支猴痘病例,且病例中越来越多没有近期国际旅行史或已知与旅行者有关联。对于大多数美国人群而言,I分支猴痘的威胁仍然较低(N17,N20,N23,N24,N28,N36,N38,N43)。

A high priorityPublic-health news

Global Monkeypox: Current Situation - cdc.gov

News Brief
The CDC's September 25, 2026, report outlines the ongoing global monkeypox outbreak, which began in 2022 and has caused over 100,000 cases in 122 countries, including 115 where it was previously unreported. The outbreak is primarily driven by clade IIb monkeypox, which has a low mortality rate (less than 0.1%). In the U.S., since November 2024, more than 60 laboratory-confirmed cases of clade I monkeypox have been reported across at least 20 states, with a growing number of cases linked to domestic transmission. These cases are mostly among gay, bisexual, or other men who have sex with men, and many were associated with travel from Central and Eastern Africa or sexual contact with individuals who had traveled. The CDC emphasizes that the risk to most people in the U.S. remains low for clade I monkeypox, while clade II continues to circulate. The agency is actively monitoring cases, providing vaccination recommendations, and collaborating with international partners to contain the outbreak. CDC also enhances early detection through surveillance systems, including wastewater testing, and offers technical support to jurisdictions. The report highlights the importance of vaccination as a key tool in preventing the spread of monkeypox, especially for those at risk or traveling to affected areas.
Original source text (English)

What to know There are two types of the virus that causes monkeypox, clade I and clade II. Both types can be spread, treated, and prevented in the same ways. Each clade has two subclades (clade Ia and clade Ib; clade IIa and IIb). Recent outbreaks of monkeypox have spread initially through intimate or sexual contact. In some cases, monkeypox was then spread within households. Recent travel-associated clade I monkeypox cases have almost exclusively been attributed to subclade Ib; there have been no deaths associated with these cases and most people had relatively mild illness. The ongoing global outbreak of clade IIb monkeypox that began in 2022 has caused more than 100,000 cases in 122 total countries, including 115 countries where monkeypox was not previously reported. CDC has vaccination recommendations for people at risk in the United States or who are traveling to countries with clade I outbreaks or new clade II outbreaks. Current situation In the United States Clade I monkeypox The risk posed by the clade I monkeypox outbreak to most people within the United States remains low. In the United States, since November 2024, there have been more than 60 laboratory-confirmed cases of clade I monkeypox in at least 20 states. The majority of these cases are among gay, bisexual, or other men who have sex with men. Many of these cases: Had recently traveled from areas associated with the outbreak in Central and Eastern Africa; Reported sexual contact during travel to parts of Western Europe where newer outbreaks are being reported; OR Reported sexual contact with people who had traveled from these areas. Since August 2026, an increasing proportion of clade I cases in the United States have been reported in individuals with no recent international travel or known links to people who had recently traveled. Cases with presumed domestic transmission have been reported in more than 10 jurisdictions, indicating a likely transition to sustained clade Ib transmission in the United States CDC expects to see additional cases of both travel-related and domestically acquired cases of clade Ib occurring across the United States. CDC continues to monitor both clade II and clade I monkeypox cases across the United States and follow surveillance reports to monitor for any changes in demographics, disease severity, or case counts by jurisdiction. CDC also continues surveillance testing to identify monkeypox clade for all specimens sent to CDC's poxvirus laboratory. Spotlight Jurisdictions can contact poxvirus@cdc.gov for CDC technical support and clinical consultations for severe cases Monday-Friday, 8 a.m.-5 p.m. Clade II monkeypox Clade II monkeypox is still circulating at low levels. Fall 2025 marked the highest monthly case counts since the peak of the outbreak in 2022. Reminder Vaccination is an important tool in stopping the spread of monkeypox. If you have certain risk factors that make you eligible, you can help protect yourself from monkeypox by getting the 2-dose monkeypox vaccine. Monkeypox Vaccination Across the globe Clade I monkeypox Outbreaks of clade I monkeypox in Central and Eastern Africa continue. Beginning in Fall 2025, several countries in Western Europe began reporting clade Ib monkeypox cases among individuals who had no documented history of international travel. These cases were likely related to male-to-male sexual contact. Other travel-associated cases of clade I monkeypox have been reported from other parts of Africa, as well as Asia, Europe, the Middle East, North America, South America, and Australia. Recent monkeypox outbreaks have low death rates During recent outbreaks, death rates ranged from than less than 0.5% (clade Ib) to about 3% (clade Ia). Clade II is the type that caused the ongoing global outbreak that began in 2022. Fewer than 0.1% of people infected with clade II die from the disease. Clade II monkeypox For global case data for clade I and clade II monkeypox since January 1, 2024, see Global Mpox Trends. Confirmed cases include those that are laboratory confirmed as monkeypox virus and may include cases only confirmed as orthopoxvirus. These data are provided for situational awareness and are subject to change. Clade I Monkeypox Outbreaks Cases of clade I monkeypox have been diagnosed worldwide. Clade II Monkeypox Outbreaks Clade II monkeypox is spreading in the United States and around the world. What CDC is doing CDC raises awareness for U.S. healthcare providers, including the latest guidance for diagnosing monkeypox and clinical decision-making. CDC continues critical work to protect the United States from emerging and infectious diseases. CDC has collaborated closely with key partners in affected countries to help support efforts to stop monkeypox at the source. CDC works closely with U.S. state, tribal, local, and territorial public health departments to provide recommendations for clinical management, diagnosis, and prevention of domestic monkeypox cases. in the U.S. CDC continually increases capacity in communities across the United States for early detection of monkeypox through existing surveillance systems, including wastewater testing. CDC raises awareness for healthcare providers, including the latest guidance for diagnosing monkeypox and clinical decision-making. CDC collaborated with governmental and civil society partners in affected countries to collect and analyze case data, and to identify how monkeypox is spreading. CDC coordinated technical assistance in response to urgent in-country needs, including: increasing the number of monkeypox testing sites; improving specimen transport networks to quickly identify new cases; assisting with Ministry of Health-led vaccine implementation; strengthening emergency management systems; improving case surveillance; and training healthcare workers. CDC's staff throughout Africa provide critical, real-time information to inform U.S. preparedness efforts. In the United States CDC works closely with state, tribal, local, and territorial public health departments to provide recommendations for clinical management, diagnosis, and prevention of monkeypox cases in the U.S. CDC continually increases capacity in communities across the United States for early detection of monkeypox through existing surveillance systems, including wastewater testing. CDC raises awareness for healthcare providers, including the latest guidance for diagnosing monkeypox and clinical decision-making. CDC has information and recommendations for members of the public, including travelers: Destinations | Travelers' Health, Travel Health Notices, and Health Alert Network advisories. CDC continues to work with international partners to help reduce monkeypox transmission. DR Congo Confirms First Mpox Disease Case - Voice of Nigeria

News Brief
Voice of Nigeria reported on 26 September 2026 that the Democratic Republic of Congo has confirmed its first case of mpox, marking the virus's initial detection in the country. The announcement confirms a single case, with no deaths or further spread mentioned. Details about the patient, the location within the DRC, or any public‐health actions such as isolation, contact tracing, or treatment were not provided. Consequently, the scale of the outbreak remains limited to this solitary case, and the risk assessment is undefined. Authorities are presumed to be investigating, but the report offers no specifics on response measures or additional epidemiological data.

Phase II Clinical Trial of NV-387 as a Treatment for Monkeypox (MPox) Has Begun in DRC, Announces NanoViricides - Yahoo Finance
新闻简报纳米病毒公司(NanoViricides,Inc.)宣布在其新型抗病毒药物NV-387的II期临床试验中,开始针对猴痘(猴痘)病毒感染的治疗研究。该试验于2026年9月23日启动,并在刚果民主共和国(DRC)招募了首批患者。该试验设计为一项适应性、国际多中心、随机、开放标签、干预性、平行组研究,旨在评估该药物在临床表现为猴痘疾病且实验室确认或疑似MPXV感染的患者中的疗效和安全性。截至2026年1月,数据安全与监测委员会(DSMB)在前50名患者随机分配后未发现新的安全性问题。该试验计划在2026年上半年再招募50名患者,以进行中期疗效分析。在提供的证据中,未报告具体病例数、死亡数或风险等级。该试验是纳米病毒公司更广泛努力的一部分,旨在开发治疗用于治疗传染病,包括猴痘。

纳米病毒公司宣布在刚果民主共和国启动NV-387作为治疗治疗猴痘(猴痘)病毒的II期临床试验,并招募了首批患者。该试验由纳米病毒公司(NanoViricides,Inc.)进行。

该试验正在进行中,前50名患者已随机分配,未发现新的安全性问题。该试验的设计包括适应性、国际多中心、随机、开放标签、干预性、平行组结构。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Phase II Clinical Trial of NV-387 as a Treatment for Monkeypox (MPox) Has Begun in DRC, Announces NanoViricides - Yahoo Finance

News Brief
NanoViricides, Inc. has announced the initiation of a Phase II clinical trial for its novel antiviral drug NV-387, aimed at treating Monkeypox (MPox) virus infection. The trial began on September 23, 2026, with the enrollment of the first patients in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC). The trial is designed as an adaptive, international, multi-center, randomized, open-label, interventional, parallel group study to evaluate the drug's efficacy and safety in patients with clinical signs of mpox disease and laboratory-confirmed or presumptive MPXV infection. As of January 2026, the Data Safety and Monitoring Board (DSMB) did not identify any new safety concerns after the first 50 patients were randomized. The trial plans to enroll an additional 50 patients in the first half of 2026 for interim efficacy analysis. No specific case counts, death counts, or risk levels were reported in the supplied evidence. The trial is part of NanoViricides' broader efforts to develop treatments for infectious diseases, including mpox.

Phase II Clinical Trial of NV-387 as a Treatment for Monkeypox (MPox) Has Begun in DRC, Announces NanoViricides ACCESS Newswire · NanoViricides NanoViricides Wed, September 23, 2026 at 8:30 AM EDT 14 min read 1 NNVC 0.00% SHELTON, CT / ACCESS Newswire / September 23, 2026 / NanoViricides, Inc. (NYSE American:NNVC) (the "Company") today announced that the Phase II Clinical Trial of the Company's Novel Broad-Spectrum Antiviral Drug NV-387 as a Treatment for Monkeypox Virus Infection has begun with enrollment of first patients in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC). This clinical trial was registered in the Pan African Clinical Trials Registry database (pactr.samrc.ac.za). The unique identification number for this clinical trial is PACTR202609506749917. The clinical trial is entitled: "An adaptive international, multi-center, randomized, open-label, interventional, parallel group, phase II (IIa and IIb) clinical trial to evaluate the efficacy and safety of a new chemical entity NV-387 formulated as oral gummies in comparison to standard of care, administered in patients with clinical signs of mpox disease and laboratory confirmed or presumptive hmpxv infection." The Principal Investigator for this clinical trial is Prof. Vivi Maketa Tevuzula, MD, MSc, PhD, Professor in the Department of Tropical Medicine at the University of Kinshasa and Principal Investigator at the Institut Multisectoriel pour l'Amélioration du Bien-être (IMABE), Kinshasa, DRC. Her work focuses on infectious diseases, including clinical trials demonstrating the safety and efficacy of novel drugs, and safety, immunogenicity, and efficacy of vaccines, as well as health systems research in resource-limited settings. She has served as principal investigator on multiple international studies on malaria, Mpox, and vaccine-preventable diseases, and leads interdisciplinary research on genomic surveillance and One Health approaches. Bayer Foundation awarded Dr. Vivi Maketa with the prestigious Early Excellence in Science Award 2023 in Medical Science, recognizing her exceptional contributions to the design and implementation of research projects on infectious and neglected tropical diseases i. "We are pleased to have Professor Maketa lead this clinical trial," said Anil R. Diwan, PhD, President and Executive Chairman of the Company, adding, "This is the very first Phase II clinical trial of the broad-spectrum antiviral drug NV-387 and aims to explore its safety and effectiveness in Mpox." There is no approved drug for Mpox. Clinical trials of a drug called tecovirimat (TPOXX, SIGA) have previously failed to demonstrate efficacy against both Mpox Clade II and Mpox Clade I. Tecovirimat was approved by US FDA for Smallpox under the Animal Rule provision. Story Continues A pan-African clinical trial called "MOSA" of a drug called brincidofovir, which is approved by US FDA for Smallpox under the Animal Rule provision, that started in January, 2025, is currently ongoing. As of January 2026, according to a press release, this study's Data Safety and Monitoring Board (DSMB) did not identify any new safety concern after the first 50 patients were randomized, and the trial plans to enrol a further 50 patients in the first half of 2026, to perform interim efficacy analysis of brincidofovir ii. Brincidofovir requires constant physician care due to dose-limiting liver and gastro-intestinal toxicity concerns iii. NV-387 could become the "go to" pandemic response drug if it is successful in this Phase II MPox clinical trial, and becomes a FDA-licensed (approved) drug. US Government SNS stockpiling contracts for existing smallpox drugs TPOXX and TEMBEXA have been in several hundreds of millions of dollars, representing an equivalent potential opportunity for NV-387. The Phase II clinical trial for NV-387 as a treatment of mpox is being conducted at Lodja in Sankuru province in DRC. As of now, this province is not an ebola-affected region. Professor Maketa's team is already on site in DRC. In addition, The team of our CRO from India, Om Sai Clinical Research Pvt. Ltd., is also on site in DRC to start the clinical trial. Lodja is a remote location and mpox cases are continuing to occur in that area. This is a resource-poor region. Setting up the clinical sites required several months of effort because of the resource limitations at the hospital. MPox Clade I is endemic in DRC and all cases in the clinical trial are expected to be of the Clade I virus. The other prominent MPox virus, MPox Clade II is substantially less severe an infection than MPox Clade I. MPox Clade II has become endemic in the USA, circulating at low levels. It primarily affects a limited population of Men-having-Sex-with-Men (MSM), because of transmission during sexual activity. MPox is an "Orphan Disease" in the USA. NanoViricides has applied to the US FDA for Orphan Drug Designation (ODD) of NV-387 for the treatment of MPox. This ODD, assuming it is granted, would enable several benefits including frequent meetings with FDA, waiver of certain FDA fees, certain R&D credits, as well as extension in exclusivity in marketing once approved. These ODD benefits can have a positive economic impact for NanoViricides estimated in the range of tens of millions of dollars. MPox Clade I cases in the USA have been slowly increasing. As of August 27, 2026, since November 2024, there have been more than 50 confirmed cases of Mpox Clade I in the USA, all of which were either travelers to Mpox-manifesting countries or regions, or contacts of such travelers, according to the CDC iv. Community spread of the MPXV Clade I is likely occurring, with 3 cases of MPox Clade I with no travel to Africa, in California in unconnected persons, according to the CDC v. However, the potential for a widespread outbreak remains low. From 2023-2025, about 1,700-2,800 cases of Mpox Clade II were confirmed in the USA, mostly occurring in men-having-sex-with men and associated sexual partners, according to the CDC (ibid #4). Clade II is transmitted via skin abrasions. Thus MPox is becoming important in the USA from the perspective of pandemic preparedness and response. Although there is a vaccine originally developed for smallpox, namely, Jynneos, that is in use to prevent MPox (primarily in clade II contacts), its immune protection was found to wane rapidly in a clinical study vi. The effectiveness of this vaccine is limited, at 36% for one dose and 66% for 2 doses against the less pathogenic MPox Clade II vii. The vaccine effectiveness is likely to be much less against the more severe MPox Clade I. Vaccines do not protect in the first few weeks, limiting their usefulness during pandemic. We believe that there will be a strong opportunity for NV-387 for pandemic preparedness and response for the threats of Mpox and Smallpox in the USA if this Phase II clinical trial of NV-387 for the treatment of Mpox is successful. The two drugs in the USA Strategic National Stockpile (SNS), TPOXX and TEMBEXA, would be unsuitable for pandemic response if MPox Clade I spreads. Although both of these drugs are approved for Smallpox, a bioterrorism agent, under the FDA animal rule, the clinical trial failure of tecovirimat against Mpox which is a much less severe and far less lethal disease compared to Smallpox raises questions about its possible utility in a Smallpox bioterrorism event. In addition, the known toxicity profile and warnings for brincidofovir make it unsuitable for wide-scale deployment in a large outbreak scenario. "NV-387, our broad-spectrum antiviral drug is poised to cause a revolution in treatment of viral diseases, just as antibiotics revolutionized the treatment of bacterial diseases," said Anil R. Diwan, Ph.D., adding "NV-387 is designed to mimic human cells to trap and destroy the virus. This single drug can target over 90-95% of human pathogenic viruses due to this biomimicry, which is reminiscent of the antibiotic penicillin that targets a large number of human pathogenic bacteria." NV-387 was found to possess strong antiviral activity against an orthopoxvirus in an animal model that is considered an important model to establish potential effectiveness against MPox and Smallpox viruses, as all of these viruses belong to the same family of orthopoxviruses. In fact, NV-387 effectiveness matched the effectiveness of the small chemical drug tecovirimat in two different models of infection, one was direct skin infection, and the other was a direct lung infection, by the virus. Escape of virus from tecovirimat is known to occur by a single point mutation in a viral protein called VP-37. Vaccines, antibodies, and small chemical drugs such as tecovirimat for MPox/Smallpox, or oseltamivir (Tamiflu®), baloxavir (Xofluza®) for Influenza are readily escaped by viruses simply by introduction of small changes that viruses undergo when they are faced with these challenges in the field. In contrast, escape of virus from NV-387 is highly unlikely because no matter how much the virus changes in the field, it continues to use sulfated proteoglycans such as HSPG as "attachment receptor" in order to cause cell infection. NV-387 mimics the sulfated proteoglycan signature feature that the viruses require. NV-387 is a host-mimetic drug that "look...
View five news elements
Time

女子在Pims检测出猴痘阳性 - 黎明

Woman tests positive for Mpox at Pims - Dawn
新闻简报一名来自巴基斯坦伊斯兰堡的41岁女性于2026年9月22日在巴基斯坦医学研究院(Pims)检测出猴痘阳性。她在面部出现皮肤病变后被送往医院的传染病控制病房。国家卫生研究院(NIH)确认了该患者体内存在猴痘病毒,目前患者病情正在好转,已脱离危险。猴痘是一种罕见的人兽共患病,通过密切接触传播,可引起类似流感的症状和脓包状皮肤病变。该疾病于2022年7月被世界卫生组织(WHO)宣布为全球卫生紧急事件,并于2022年11月更名为猴痘,以应对污名化问题。虽然病毒的I类群会导致更严重的疾病并伴有更高的死亡率死亡率,但近期流行的II类群则导致较轻的疾病,其生存率超过99.9%。该病例凸显了隔离和规范病例管理在防止病毒进一步传播中的重要性。该信息由医院和NIH确认,来源为权威新闻机构。

时间

该病例报告于巴基斯坦伊斯兰堡,涉及一名41岁的女性患者,被送往巴基斯坦医学研究院(Pims)治疗。

一名41岁的女性被送往Pims后,因面部出现皮肤病变而检测出猴痘阳性。国家卫生研究院(NIH)确认了该患者体内存在该病毒。
规模、影响与风险
患者病情正在好转,已脱离危险。猴痘是一种罕见的病毒性人兽共患病,虽然I类群可能导致严重疾病和死亡,但II类群则较为温和,生存率超过99.9%。该疾病通过密切接触传播,并与污名化和种族歧视相关。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Woman tests positive for Mpox at Pims - Dawn

News Brief
A 41-year-old woman from Islamabad, Pakistan, tested positive for Mpox at the Pakistan Institute of Medical Sciences (Pims) on September 22, 2026. She was admitted to the hospital’s Infectious Control Ward after developing skin lesions on her face. The National Institute of Health (NIH) confirmed the presence of the Mpox virus in the patient, who is currently improving and out of danger. Mpox, a rare zoonotic disease, spreads through close contact and can cause flu-like symptoms and pus-filled skin lesions. The disease was declared a global health emergency by the WHO in July 2022 and renamed from monkeypox to Mpox in November 2022 to address stigma. While clade I of the virus is more severe and associated with higher mortality rates, clade II, which is prevalent in recent outbreaks, causes less severe illness with a survival rate of over 99.9%. The patient’s case highlights the importance of isolation and proper case management to prevent further spread. The information is confirmed by the hospital and NIH, and the source is a reputable news outlet.
Original source text (English)
EPAPER LIVE TV DAWNNEWS URDU Images Herald Aurora CityFM89 Advertise Events Supplements Careers Obituaries Dawn E-Paper | September 28, 2026 Home Latest Violations Tracker Pakistan Opinion Business Images Prism World Sport Breathe Magazines Tech Videos Popular Archive Flood Donations Home Latest Violations Tracker Pakistan Opinion Business Images Prism World Sport Breathe Magazines Tech Videos Popular Archive Flood Donations Search Search Subscribe to notifications Get the latest news and updates from Dawn Not Now Allow Notifications Woman tests positive for Mpox at Pims Ikram Junaidi Published September 22, 2026 Email Your Name* Recipient Email* Cancel 0 A test tube labelled "Mpox virus positive" is held in this illustration taken August 20, 2024. — Reuters/File Listen to article 1x 1.2x 1.5x comments Join our Whatsapp Channel Add Dawn as a trusted source ISLAMABAD: A 41-year-old woman admitted to the Pakistan Institute of Medical Sciences (Pims) has tested positive for Mpox. According to a doctor at the hospital, an Islamabad resident was brought to Pims last week after developing skin lesions on her face. “She was admitted to the hospital’s Infectious Control Ward. As she had symptoms of Mpox, samples were collected from her and sent to the National Institute of Health (NIH). The NIH has confirmed the presence of Mpox in the patient,” the doctor said. However, he said the patient was out of danger and was improving. It is worth mentioning that Mpox, which spreads through close contact and tends to cause flu-like symptoms and pus-filled skin lesions, was declared a global health emergency by the WHO in July 2022. In November of the same year, the organisation named the disease Mpox to replace the older term monkeypox, citing concerns over stigma and racism associated with the name. Mpox is a rare viral zoonotic disease caused by infection with the Mpox virus. Although the natural reservoir of Mpox remains unknown, African rodents and non-human primates, such as monkeys, may harbour the virus and infect people. The patient develops a rash within one to three days after the appearance of fever, often beginning on the face and then spreading to other parts of the body. “There are two types of Mpox virus: clade I (Congo Basin) and clade II (West African). Clade I, which is more associated with recent outbreaks, causes more severe illness and deaths. Some outbreaks have killed up to 10pc of the people who get sick, although more recent outbreaks have had lower death rates. Clade I is endemic to Central Africa. Clade II is the type involved in the global outbreak since 2022. Infections from clade II Mpox are less severe. More than 99.9pc of people survive,” an advisory issued by NIH stated. According to the advisory, transmission occurs through contact with an infected animal, human, or materials contaminated with the virus. The virus enters the body through broken skin, even if not visible, the respiratory tract, or the mucous membranes, including the eyes, nose, or mouth. Other human-to-human methods of transmission include direct or indirect contact with body fluids or lesion material, or through contaminated clothing or linens. Case management of a confirmed Mpox patient involves several steps to ensure proper treatment and prevent the spread of the virus, including isolation.
