当印度警惕边境病毒时,猴痘在喀拉拉邦和泰伦加纳邦留下了安静的足迹——《南方第一》

While India watched its borders for virus, mpox left a quiet trail in Kerala and Telangana - The South First
新闻简报由印度ICMR领导的一项研究于2023年9月至2024年3月期间进行,发现喀拉拉邦和泰伦加纳邦的关键人群中14名个体有先前猴痘暴露的证据。这些参与者年龄在18至40岁之间,是更大群体1,790名个体的一部分。他们均无近期旅行、接触疑似猴痘患者或动物暴露史,其血液样本显示IgG抗体,表明有既往暴露但无近期感染。该研究强调,IgM抗体的缺失表明没有正在进行的社区传播传播。研究结果突显了传统监测方法的局限性,并强调需要更针对性的方法来检测无症状或轻症病例。研究人员还指出,抗体检测并不能确认免疫力,且研究结果无法确定暴露的具体时间和途径。这项研究有助于了解印度猴痘的暴露模式,特别是在有国际旅行联系的地区。
查看新闻五要素
时间
本研究于2023年9月至2024年3月期间进行,发表日期为2026-09-22。事件日期未明确说明,但研究结果指的是之前的暴露模式。
地点与对象
该研究聚焦于印度喀拉拉邦和泰伦加纳邦的关键人群,共有1,790名年龄在18至40岁之间的参与者。参与者包括男性和跨性别者。
事件
由ICMR领导的一项研究发现,喀拉拉邦和泰伦加纳邦的关键人群中14名参与者有先前猴痘暴露的证据。研究结果表明是零星的先前暴露,而非正在进行的社区传播传播。
规模、影响与风险
在1,790名参与者中,有14人检测出针对猴痘的IgG抗体,代表0.78%的患病率率。研究未发现正在进行的社区传播或近期感染的证据,因为这些参与者均未检测到IgM抗体。研究期间,所调查人群中未显示有活跃传播的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究强调需要有针对性的哨兵监测来检测轻症或无症状感染。然而,它无法确定暴露的具体时间和途径。研究结果并未确认正在进行的暴发,但引发了对当前监测系统有效性的疑问。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

While India watched its borders for virus, mpox left a quiet trail in Kerala and Telangana - The South First

News Brief
An ICMR-led study conducted between September 2023 and March 2024 found evidence of previous mpox exposure in 14 individuals from key populations in Kerala and Telangana, India. These participants, aged 18 to 40, were part of a larger group of 1,790 individuals. None of them had recent travel, contact with suspected mpox patients, or animal exposure, and their blood samples showed IgG antibodies, indicating prior exposure but not recent infections. The study emphasized that the absence of IgM antibodies suggests no ongoing community transmission. The findings highlight the limitations of conventional surveillance methods and the need for more targeted approaches to detect asymptomatic or mild cases. The researchers also noted that antibody detection does not confirm immunity, and the results could not determine the exact timing or route of exposure. This study contributes to understanding mpox exposure patterns in India, particularly in regions with international travel links.
Original source text (English)
While India watched its borders for virus, mpox left a quiet trail in Kerala and Telangana Periodic sentinel serosurveys and strengthened laboratory capacity will remain important for monitoring changes in exposure patterns and informing future vaccination and preparedness strategies in the context of evolving global mpox epidemiology. Sumit Jha Published Sep 22, 2026 | 7:00 AM ⚊ Updated Sep 22, 2026 | 7:00 AM Representative image of the mpox virus (Creative Commons) Make Us Your Preferred Source on Google Synopsis: An ICMR-led study has found evidence of previous mpox exposure among 14 of 1,790 participants from key populations across India, including Keralam and Telangana. While the study found no ongoing community transmission, the findings suggested conventional travel- and symptom-based surveillance may miss mild or asymptomatic infections, highlighting the need for targeted sentinel surveillance. Fourteen people gave blood samples for an Indian Council of Medical Research (ICMR)-led study. None of them had travelled overseas or had contact with a suspected mpox patient. None reported recent animal exposure. Yet their blood carried antibodies suggesting previous exposure to mpox (previously known as monkeypox) or a closely related virus. The finding was from a multicentric study that detected anti-mpox immunoglobulin G (IgG) antibodies in 14 of 1,790 people, or 0.78%, from key populations across India. The sample size was small, and the study found no evidence of ongoing community transmission. Still, the finding raised a larger question about whether India’s existing surveillance system could detect infections that occur outside the travel-linked and symptomatic cases. Researchers sought to “assess the prevalence of anti-MPXV (monkeypox virus) IgG and Immunoglobulin M (IgM) antibodies and identify socio-demographic and behavioural determinants of seropositivity among key populations in India”. Also Read: Mpox manifests like chickenpox: How to tell the difference India first watched mpox through travel and symptoms Mpox, a viral disease that spreads between animals and humans and between people, first emerged in India’s surveillance system on 14 July 2022, when the country detected its first case in a United Arab Emirates-returnee in Keralam. He survived. Keralam subsequently accounted for a substantial share of India’s reported cases, with its international travel links, particularly to the Middle East, playing a role in detecting imported infections. The state also reported India’s first mpox casualty on 30 July 2022. Most infections identified during the 2022 global outbreak belonged to the Clade IIb strain. India later augmented airport screening, contact tracing, isolation facilities and laboratory preparedness, particularly after the World Health Organisation declared the mpox outbreak in Africa a public health emergency again in 2024. The ICMR study took a different angle. Instead of asking who had arrived from an affected country or who turned up at a hospital with symptoms, researchers looked for evidence of previous exposure in people from key populations who the healthcare system may not have seen. The study was conducted between September 2023 and March 2024 through seven community-based organisations. It included 1,790 participants aged 18 to 40 years. The researchers selected this age group because they were born after India discontinued routine smallpox vaccination. “This specific age range was selected because it represents the cohort born after the cessation of routine smallpox vaccination in India,” the researchers said. The researchers excluded participants with a visible smallpox vaccination scar. “Participants were examined for the presence of a smallpox vaccination scar, and individuals bearing such a scar were excluded from the study,” the authors said. It was done to reduce the possibility that the detected antibodies were related to smallpox vaccination. Also Read: UAE reported first ‘official’ mpox case in February 2025 Fourteen people carried an mpox antibody signal Of the 1,790 participants, 14 tested positive for IgG antibodies against mpox. IgG antibodies can remain in the blood after a previous exposure to an infection. But none of the participants had IgM antibodies, which indicated a more recent infection. The absence of IgM “indicates that infections were not recent and provides no evidence of ongoing community transmission during the study period”, the researchers said. It meant the findings did not indicate that an mpox outbreak was actively spreading among the surveyed population during the study period. Instead, it pointed to a smaller and more difficult question: whether some people had encountered the virus earlier without being diagnosed. The researchers described the finding as “sporadic prior exposure rather than sustained community transmission”. Also Read: India’s first mpox clade 1b strain detected in Kerala Keralam, Telangana carry the signal Keralam recorded five IgG-positive samples among 242 participants, tying with Punjab for the highest number of positives. Telangana recorded two positives among 241 participants. The National Capital Region of Delhi recorded one among 292, while Mizoram recorded one among 198. None of the samples from Maharashtra, West Bengal or Nagaland returned IgG-positive. The two Telangana participants were men aged 26 and 34. Both tested positive for mpox IgG antibodies. When tested for whether their antibodies could block mpox, neither sample showed that activity. Instead, both showed antibodies capable of neutralising the closely related Vaccinia virus. The finding was significant because viruses from the same orthopoxvirus family could trigger similar antibody responses. The researchers explained that “due to the conserved antigenic structure among orthopoxviruses, particularly shared surface proteins and epitopes, there will be serological cross-reactivity to some extent”. In simple terms, a positive antibody test does not automatically ascertain that a person had mpox. The researchers also cautioned against interpreting an antibody-positive result as proof of immunity, saying, “Detection of IgG antibodies does not necessarily indicate protective immunity”. Also Read: Should India be concerned about mpox? No travel, known contact or animal exposure Among the 14 people who tested positive, 12 were aged 31-40 years, and two were in the 18-30 age group. Eleven were men or transgender participants. Eleven of the 14 participants had been sexually active within 21 days before their samples were collected. Among those who answered questions about their recent sexual activity, “70% (7/10) reported having 2-5 sexual partners in the preceding month”, the researchers said. But researchers found no further obvious exposure routes. “None reported recent contact with suspected mpox cases, international travel, or animal exposure within the prior 21 days”, the researchers said. The study could not establish when these people were exposed or how they contracted the virus, if it were mpox. But the absence of a reported travel or known-contact history was significant because India’s mpox surveillance had historically detected many cases through travel-related screening and asymptomatic people. Also Read: Experts seek local-transmission proof India has largely watched for cases at the border and in clinics The ICMR paper noted that India reported 66 confirmed mpox cases at the time of the study’s analysis, with most linked to international travel. It also stated that sustained community transmission had not been confirmed. The study, however, looked at a different part of the larger picture. “In India, most available mpox data are derived from laboratory-confirmed symptomatic cases detected through point-of-entry screening or clinical referrals”, the authors said. Such an approach could miss people who never developed obvious symptoms or never sought medical care. The researchers noted that many mpox infections during recent outbreaks have been mild, atypical or asymptomatic, meaning clinical surveillance alone may underestimate previous exposure. The study therefore described its findings as “sporadic prior exposure rather than sustained community transmission”. The distinction was important. The researchers were not reporting a hidden mpox outbreak. They were identifying a small number of antibody signals that conventional surveillance might have missed. Also Read: What is monkeypox? What are its symptoms? Why vulnerable populations matter The survey was conducted through community organisations that run sexual health clinics and counselling services for key populations, including gay and transgender people. The researchers identified “interconnected social networks, higher prevalence of sexually transmitted infections, and barriers to healthcare access” among the factors that made these populations important for mpox surveillance. It mattered because an infection causing mild symptoms, or no obvious symptoms, may never reach a healthcare facility and therefore may not enter conventional disease surveillance. The researchers said some infections “could have been asymptomatic or mild and therefore missed by conventional surveillance systems”. At the same time, the study did not suggest widespread transmission among these communities. The authors said, “the absence of IgM positivity and the very low overall seroprevalence provide no evidence of widespread ongoing community circulation during the study period”. Also Read: Virus had half a century journey to global health emergency What the second test tells us The researchers subjected all 14 antibody-positive samples to another laboratory test called Plaque Reduction Neutralisation Test (PRNT) 50. PRNT50 checks whether antibodies in a person’s blood can block a virus from infecting cells in a laboratory. It therefore provides additional information beyond a basic antibody test. Nine of the 14 samples showed this virus-blocking activity against mpox. Five did not. Thirteen of the 14 showed similar activity against Vaccinia virus. The researchers explained the importance of both tests, saying “these results indicate that while IgG ELISA detected binding antibodies in all cases, PRNT50 identified functional neutralising antibodies in a subset, highlighting the added value of combining serological and neutralisation assays to assess true protective immunity”. The five samples that had mpox IgG, but did not block the disease in laboratory conditions, could represent weakening immunity or antibodies that recognise the virus without being able to neutralise it. The researchers said this “suggests possible waning immunity or low-level non-neutralising binding antibodies”. The authors cautioned that PRNT50 “may not completely distinguish virus-specific neutralisation from cross-neutralising responses among closely related Orthopoxviruses”. They added that “some degree of overestimation of true MPXV exposure cannot be excluded despite the use of confirmatory neutralisation assays”. Also Read: Experts warn against stigma as WHO declares monkeypox a public health emergency What India could change The researchers argued that antibody surveys should complement existing mpox surveillance rather than replace it. They said periodic sentinel surveys “could complement existing surveillance systems and help identify changes in mpox exposure patterns”. The researchers also pointed to India’s existing disease surveillance and laboratory infrastructure. The paper recommended “integrating mpox surveillance with systems such as the Integrated Disease Surveillance Programme and the Virus Research and Diagnostic Laboratory network to strengthen early detection and outbreak preparedness”. The study also demonstrated the value of reaching communities through organisations already working with them rather than relying entirely on people to present at hospitals. The authors noted that “use of community-based organisations enabled access to key populations that are often underserved and difficult to reach through conventional surveillance mechanisms”. The researchers concluded that “Periodic sentinel serosurveys and strengthened laboratory capacity will remain important for monitoring changes in exposure patterns and informing future vaccination and preparedness strategies in the context of evolving global mpox epidemiology.” (Edited by Majnu Babu)
View five news elements
Time
The study was conducted between September 2023 and March 2024, with the publication date on 2026-09-22. The event date is not explicitly stated, but the findings refer to previous exposure patterns.
Location and population
The study focused on key populations in Kerala and Telangana, India, with 1,790 participants aged 18 to 40 years. The participants included men and transgender individuals.
Event
An ICMR-led study found evidence of previous mpox exposure in 14 participants from key populations in Kerala and Telangana, India. The findings suggest sporadic prior exposure rather than ongoing community transmission.
Scale, impact and risk
Out of 1,790 participants, 14 tested positive for IgG antibodies against mpox, representing a 0.78% prevalence rate. The study found no evidence of ongoing community transmission or recent infections, as none had IgM antibodies. The risk of active spread among the surveyed population during the study period was not indicated.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study highlights the need for targeted sentinel surveillance to detect mild or asymptomatic infections. However, it could not establish the exact timing or route of exposure. The findings do not confirm an ongoing outbreak but raise questions about the effectiveness of current surveillance systems.

Mpox in South Africa: What you need to know about symptoms, spread, and treatment - iol.co.za

News Brief
The article from iol.co.za, published on September 23, 2026, discusses the rising mpox cases in South Africa, with 22 laboratory-confirmed infections reported across three provinces: the Western Cape (18 cases), Gauteng (3 cases), and KwaZulu-Natal (1 case). All but one of the patients are male, aged between 23 and 50. The latest cases include a 47-year-old Cape Town man who recently traveled to Spain and was initially classified as a probable case. Health authorities are conducting contact tracing and enhanced surveillance, particularly in the Western Cape, where preliminary analysis suggests the involvement of Clade Ib mpox virus. The Department of Health has noted that mpox is not easily spread through casual contact but mainly through close physical contact with infected individuals or contaminated objects. Symptoms typically include fever, headache, muscle aches, and a rash that progresses through various stages. While there is no routine cure for mild mpox, Tecovirimat is available in limited supply for severe cases. The article emphasizes the importance of seeking medical attention if symptoms appear after close contact with a confirmed or suspected case.

Image: Freepik Mpox cases are rising in South Africa, with health authorities now reporting 22 laboratory-confirmed infections across three provinces. The latest increase has understandably raised questions about how the virus spreads, what symptoms to look for and what to do if you think you have been exposed. Here is what you need to know about mpox in South Africa, including the latest local situation, symptoms, treatment and ways to reduce your risk. The local context: Why Mpox is in the news South Africa has recorded 22 laboratory-confirmed mpox cases, according to the Department of Health's latest update. The Western Cape accounts for 18 cases, with three in Gauteng and one in KwaZulu-Natal. All but one of the 22 patients are male, with those affected ranging in age from 23 to 50. The latest cases include a 47-year-old Cape Town man who recently travelled to Spain. He had initially been classified as a probable case while South African authorities awaited laboratory results from Spanish health authorities. The other three newly confirmed cases include two imported infections involving a German and a Norwegian citizen. Health authorities and the National Institute for Communicable Diseases (NICD) are continuing contact tracing and enhanced surveillance, particularly in the Western Cape. Preliminary analysis of the recent Western Cape clusters indicates that Clade Ib mpox virus is involved. Video Player is loading. Advertisement Next Stay Close ✕ Ad loading What exactly is Mpox? Mpox, previously known as monkeypox, is a viral disease caused by the mpox virus, which belongs to the Orthopoxvirus family. It commonly causes a rash or skin lesions, along with symptoms such as fever, headache, muscle aches, back pain, tiredness and swollen lymph nodes. Most people recover from mpox, but some can develop more serious illness. There are different groups, or clades, of the virus. Clade Ib has been involved in recent outbreaks in parts of Africa and has also been detected in other countries. You May Like I Was Tired Of Buying My Husband Gifts He’d Forget About A Week LaterResparked Learn More Undo Hardien family seeks docket after killer’s 2032 parole eligibility Undo Rental Property Investors: The 2026 DSCR Loan ChecklistBrightPath Loans Read More Undo 11 killed in head-on collision between taxi and truck on N2 in northern KZN Undo How is Mpox transmitted? Mpox is mainly spread through close physical contact with an infected person. This can include: Skin-to-skin contact with mpox lesions. Kissing or other close face-to-face contact. Sexual contact. Contact with contaminated clothing, bedding or towels. Prolonged close contact, including exposure to respiratory droplets. People can also contract mpox through contact with infected animals in areas where the virus circulates in animal populations. Mpox is not spread as easily through casual contact as common respiratory infections such as colds and flu. Close contact remains the main route of transmission. Spotting the signs: Symptoms and what the rash looks like Symptoms usually appear within 5 to 21 days after exposure, according to the Department of Health. Common symptoms include: Fever Headache Muscle aches Back pain Tiredness Swollen lymph nodes A rash or skin lesions The rash can appear on the face, hands, feet, genitals or other parts of the body. The lesions typically begin as flat red spots before becoming raised bumps, fluid-filled blisters and then pus-filled lesions. They eventually crust over and heal. Not everyone will experience exactly the same symptoms, so an unexplained rash should be assessed by a healthcare professional, particularly if there has been recent close contact with a confirmed or suspected case. Who is more at risk of severe illness? While many people recover without serious complications, some groups have a higher risk of severe mpox. These include: Young children, particularly infants. Pregnant people. People with weakened immune systems. People with advanced or uncontrolled HIV. People with certain serious skin conditions. Diagnosis, treatment and home care Mpox can resemble other illnesses, so a rash cannot always be diagnosed by appearance alone. If mpox is suspected, a healthcare professional can arrange laboratory testing, usually using a sample from a skin lesion. Anyone who develops symptoms after close contact with a confirmed or suspected case should seek medical attention and avoid close contact with others while being assessed. How is Mpox treated? There is no routine cure for mild mpox. Treatment generally focuses on managing symptoms while the body fights the infection. This can include treatment for pain and fever, keeping the skin clean and preventing secondary infections. South Africa's current outbreak response includes a limited supply of Tecovirimat, an antiviral treatment, which health authorities can use as part of their response. Its use is determined by healthcare authorities and is not a routine treatment for everyone with mpox. People who become seriously ill or who have a higher risk of complications may require hospital care. What should you do if you have symptoms? If you develop an unexplained rash, fever or other symptoms associated with mpox, particularly after close contact with someone who has the virus, contact a healthcare professional. Until you have been assessed: Avoid close physical contact with other people. Do not touch, scratch or pop lesions. Do not share towels, bedding, clothing or other personal items. Wash your hands regularly with soap and water or use hand sanitiser. Follow medical advice about staying away from others while you are infectious. Early detection and avoiding further close contact can help limit transmission. Can Mpox be prevented? Reducing close contact with someone who has mpox is one of the most important ways to lower your risk. Good hand hygiene and avoiding contact with an infected person's lesions or contaminated personal items can also help prevent spread. South Africa has also secured limited supplies of MVA-BN mpox vaccine as part of its outbreak response. Health authorities will determine where and how available doses are used, with vaccination forming part of a broader response that also includes surveillance and other prevention measures. If you have been in close contact with someone diagnosed with mpox and subsequently develop symptoms, seek medical attention promptly. Health authorities have also stressed the importance of avoiding stigma around mpox. The virus can affect anyone, and people who suspect they may have been exposed should feel able to seek medical care. IOL Lifestyle Get your news on the go. 超过三分之一的猴痘阳性检测结果可能是误诊

More than a third of positive tests for mpox may have been false diagnoses
新闻简报由日内瓦大学(UNIGE)领导的一项研究,于2024年4月至2026年4月在刚果民主共和国(DRC)进行,发现35%的猴痘 PCR阳性检测结果可能由于环境污染而出现误诊。该研究发表于《柳叶刀·传染病》杂志,揭示了猴痘病毒DNA可能在治疗中心的表面和空气中持续存在,导致在检测其他患者时出现错误结果。这一发现对公共卫生具有重要意义,因为可能导致未实际感染的个体被不必要的住院、隔离或治疗。研究建议降低PCR阳性阈值以减少假阳性结果,同时不增加假阴性结果,并在资源匮乏地区加强实验室人员培训,以便及时发现此类异常情况。世界卫生组织(WHO)于2024年8月将刚果民主共和国的猴痘 暴发列为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件,强调了准确的诊断对有效公共卫生措施的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2024年4月至2026年4月进行,研究结果于2026年9月22日发表。
地点与对象
该研究聚焦于刚果民主共和国(DRC)的四个城市:戈马(Goma)、卡米图加(Kamituga)、金沙萨(Kinshasa)和乌维拉(Uvira)。研究涉及这些地区对猴痘的PCR检测。
事件
由日内瓦大学(UNIGE)领导的国际团队发现,刚果民主共和国(DRC)中35%的猴痘 PCR阳性检测结果可能是由于样本环境污染而出现的误诊。
规模、影响与风险
该研究突显了猴痘检测中可能出现的显著假阳性诊断,这可能导致未实际感染的个体被不必要的过度治疗、住院或隔离。这对公共卫生资源分配和暴发 监测的准确性具有影响。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究建议调整PCR阳性阈值以减少假阳性并提高诊断准确性。同时,强调在资源匮乏地区需要更好地培训实验室人员,以便实时识别此类异常情况。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

More than a third of positive tests for mpox may have been false diagnoses

News Brief
A study led by the University of Geneva (UNIGE) and conducted in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) between April 2024 and April 2026 found that 35% of PCR-positive mpox tests may have been false diagnoses due to environmental contamination. The research, published in The Lancet Infectious Diseases, revealed that mpox DNA can persist on surfaces and in the air of treatment centers, leading to incorrect results when testing other patients. This finding has significant implications for public health, as it could result in unnecessary hospitalization, quarantine, or treatment for individuals without actual infection. The study recommends lowering the PCR positivity threshold to reduce false positives without increasing false negatives, and improving lab staff training in low-resource settings to detect such anomalies promptly. The WHO declared the mpox outbreak in the DRC a Public Health Emergency of International Concern in August 2024, underscoring the importance of accurate diagnosis for effective public health measures.
Original source text (English)

PCR testing plays a central role in mpox diagnosis. © Adobe Stock In August 2024, the WHO declared the mpox upsurge in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), and its spread to neighbouring countries, a Public Health Emergency of International Concern. The emergence of a new strain and its rapid spread across eastern DRC, and later to other African countries and beyond, made reliable diagnosis essential for understanding the scale of the epidemic and guide vaccination and other public health measures. A study led by the University of Geneva (UNIGE), the Institute of Tropical Medicine Antwerp (Belgium) and the National Institute of Biomedical Research (DRC), analysed more than 2,700 PCR results for mpox in four Congolese cities — Goma, Kamituga, Kinshasa, and Uvira — between April 2024 and April 2026. The findings show that 35% of samples reported as PCR-positive may not have represented true infections, but rather environmental contamination with mpox viral DNA. Published in The Lancet Infectious Diseases, the study has direct implications for outbreak surveillance and for the individuals falsely diagnosed. Mpox is a viral disease characterised by fever and a distinctive rash that can cause painful and disfiguring skin lesions and, in some cases, serious or fatal complications. Fast, reliable case detection is essential for measuring the scale of an outbreak, targeting vaccination, adjusting public health measures, and slowing transmission — particularly in resource-limited settings. PCR testing plays a central role in mpox diagnosis as clinical symptoms are not specific to this virus. But when the same unusual pattern emerged in results from several different treatment centres, the team led by Andrew Azman, associate professor at the Institute of Global Health and the Center for Emerging Viral Diseases at the UNIGE Faculty of Medicine, took notice. Instead of a continuous distribution of CT values (the indicator reflecting the amount of virus detected in a sample — the lower the value, the higher the viral load), the results clustered sharply into two distinct groups. "The first group corresponds to low values, consistent with a high viral load and therefore a genuine active infection," says Azman. "The second cluster sat just at the detection threshold, pointing to an extremely low viral load — atypical for a real infection, but typical of a residual viral trace. What's striking is that this clear split between the two groups appeared almost identically across all four sites, which is unlikely to be a coincidence." Mpox DNA is highly stable and can persist for several weeks on surfaces, or even remain in the air of treatment centres. This environmental contamination can then be picked up by mistake when sampling other patients. Contaminated surfaces To understand why, the team swabbed surfaces in the treatment areas of two care centres. Many of the surfaces tested positive for traces of the virus, while surfaces sampled outside the treatment areas came back negative. Mpox DNA is highly stable and can persist for several weeks on surfaces, or even remain in the air of treatment centres. This environmental contamination can then be picked up by mistake when sampling other patients, with no connection to an actual infection. "We also looked at serological data for these individuals where available," explains Megan O'Driscoll, the study's first author and a postdoctoral researcher at the Institute of Global Health. "89% of the people that our model predicts had false-positive diagnoses showed no trace of antibodies against the virus." In a small separate study, the team tested samples from patients with suspected mpox for multiple rash-causing viruses and found that most people had other viruses, like varicella (chickenpox) and measles, with few testing positive for mpox. Adjusting positivity threshold These false diagnoses carry real consequences — for public health resource allocation, and for the people affected: unnecessary overtreatment, hospitalisation, or quarantine at a mpox treatment centre, along with the social stigma associated with the disease, and a very real risk of contracting an actual infection there. "The simplest and fastest fix is to lower the PCR positivity threshold," Azman adds. "A lower threshold significantly reduces the number of false positives without meaningfully increasing false negatives — a proposal currently under discussion with decision-makers. In the long term, this shows that we need to improve the training of lab staff in low-resource settings like this so they can spot these anomalies in real time, rather than after the acute emergency is over." This work is part of the mandate of the Geneva Center for Emerging Viral Diseases, a WHO collaborating centre for diagnostics, which supports several countries in improving their diagnostic practices. 22 Sept 2026 Media University About the University General Policy Teaching Excellence in research Community Services International University structure Rectorate Statistics History Faculties Sciences Medicine Humanities Social Sciences Economics and Management Law Theology Psychology and Educational Sciences Translation and Interpreting Centres and Institutes Students Admission Student Exchange Bachelor Programs (in French) Master Programs Library Campus Life Help UNIGE Continuing & distance education Geneva Summer Schools Accommodation Office Services Community Services Campus Accessibility Library Archive ouverte Media and Communication Teaching services University structure Welcome center Help UNIGE Health and Safety FACULTY Who we are Organisation Dean's Office HR & Careers Partnerships Environmental sustainability Institutes and centres TEACHING Postgraduate education Continuing Education RESEARCH Clinical medicine Fundamental Medicine Dental Medicine Core facilities Research data management SmartLab-eLabNext PUBLIC OUTREACH Media Newsletter Our activities at a glance 150th anniversary AGENDA CONTACT INFORMATION Students & Teaching Administration Administration & Dean's Office Sections and Departments Conflicts and integrity Where to find us

Original source text (Chinese)
# 猴痘病毒Clade Ib与Clade IIb在兔模型中的比较致病性研究 《Virulence》:Comparative pathogenicity study of monkeypox virus Clade Ib and Clade IIb in a rabbit model 【字体: 大 中 小 】 时间:2026年09月25日 来源:Virulence 6.8 编辑推荐: Mpox已作为一项重大的公共卫生关切出现,随着新变体的出现,其传播已跨越各大洲。来自近期全球爆发的Clade IIb以及新发现的Clade Ib在传播动力学及可能的严重程度上均表现出变化。研究人员通过皮内和静脉联合接种途径,建立了一种新西兰白兔Mpox模型。感 Mpox已作为一项重大的公共卫生关切出现,随着新变体的出现,其传播已跨越各大洲。来自近期全球爆发的Clade IIb以及新发现的Clade Ib在传播动力学及可能的严重程度上均表现出变化。研究人员通过皮内和静脉联合接种途径,建立了一种新西兰白兔Mpox模型。感染后,兔皮肤出现红斑性丘疹样病变,至第二周进展为结痂和脱痂形成,最终实现愈合和血清转换。对Clade Ib和IIb.A.2.1谱系感染的研究显示,两种猴痘病毒(Monkeypox virus,MPXV)变异株均能引起皮肤和睾丸病变,以及鼻腔、皮肤和睾丸组织的病毒排出。然而,两种变异株在疾病严重程度方面无显著差异。这些发现表明,该兔模型可用于评估针对MPXV的防控措施。 # 兔模型用于MPXV Clade Ib与IIb.A.2.1致病性比较研究解读 ## 研究背景与问题 Mpox(猴痘)是由正痘病毒属(Orthopoxvirus)病原体引起的人畜共患疾病,近年来已成为日益重要的全球公共卫生问题。该疾病于1970年在刚果民主共和国首次被发现,此后数十年间主要局限于中非和西非地区,感染主要与接触这些地区感染的啮齿动物有关。然而,2022年Mpox发生全球传播,成为非洲以外报告的最大规模流行,促使世界卫生组织(WHO)于2022年7月23日至2023年5月10日宣布其为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件。与此前通常与疫区旅行史或动物接触相关的感染不同,2022年流行与个体间的性接触相关。2024年,中非和东非报告的Mpox病例再次增加,WHO于2024年8月14日第二次宣布国际关注的突发公共卫生事件。 系统发育学上,病毒分为两个不同的分支(clade):Clade I(中非或刚果盆地分支)和Clade II(西非分支)。2022年前报告的Mpox疫情平均病死率(CFR),Clade I为10.6%,Clade II低于4%。2022年病毒全球传播后,猴痘病毒(Monkeypox virus,MPXV)的加速微进化被观察到,导致分支进一步细分为亚分支(Ia, Ib, IIa, IIb)和谱系(A, B, C)。2022至2023年全球Mpox病例激增主要与Clade IIb MPXV相关,而Clade Ib则主要在2024年中非和东非感染激增期间报告。Clade IIb.B.1变异株是2022年流行期间报告的主要谱系,Clade IIb.C.1从2023年年中起成为重要变异株,Clade IIb.A.2谱系在美国、英国、印度和泰国也有报告。这些变异株基因组中出现了多种核苷酸替换和缺失,但其对基因功能的影响尚不清楚。特别是A.2变异株中观察到独特的核苷酸变化,表明其独立出现。该变异株中鉴定出OPG174基因(一个影响毒力的基因)的七核苷酸缺失。OPG174已知可抑制宿主免疫应答,其突变可能改变毒力,并可作为治疗干预的潜在靶点。 动物模型是研究病毒特性以及评估疫苗或治疗方法的重要工具。多种动物模型如实验室小鼠品系、豚鼠、睡鼠、兔、草原犬鼠、松鼠和非人灵长类模型已被研究用于MPXV易感性评估。这些模型对感染表现出不同程度的易感性,但并非所有人类Mpox疾病的临床特征都能被重现。野生啮齿动物如地松鼠、草原犬鼠、睡鼠和Cast/EiJ小鼠高度易感,但难以在世界各地获得用于研究;非人灵长类模型相对昂贵且不易获得;兔类价格较低且更易操作,已被用作兔痘和痘苗病毒等其他痘病毒的动物模型,但其与人类的免疫学和解剖学差异是一个缺点。此前两项研究已证实兔对MPXV的易感性,分别使用1976年Copenhagen株(Clade I)和Clade IIb C.1.1变异株。然而,已有动物模型研究大多使用历史分离株,针对近期分离株的研究仅限于少数几项。因此,利用更新的病毒分离株建立合适的动物模型,对于理解病毒进化后的致病性和传播动态、评估干预措施的有效性具有重要意义。 ## 研究人员开展的研究与结论 为比较2022至2023年流行期间分离的MPXV Clade Ib和Clade IIb.A.2.1病毒在兔模型中引起的疾病,研究人员开发了一种结合皮内(intradermal)和静脉(intravenous)两种途径接种的新西兰白兔(New Zealand White rabbit)模型。通过此模型,研究人员首先验证了兔对MPXV的易感性,进而对两种病毒变异株的致病性进行了平行比较。研究结果显示,两种变异株均能在兔中引起皮肤和睾丸病变,经鼻腔、皮肤和睾丸组织排毒,并能诱导特异性抗体应答。然而,两种变异株在疾病严重程度上未观察到显著差异。该研究建立了一个可用于评估针对MPXV的医疗对策的兔模型,为后续疫苗和治疗方法的评价提供了实用工具。 ## 主要关键技术方法 研究人员使用了以下主要技术方法开展研究:病毒分离与生长动力学分析,在Vero(ATCC? CCL-81?)细胞上进行病毒培养、滴定(按Reed和Muench法计算半数组织培养感染量TCID 50 ),并通过全基因组测序对病毒分离株进行序列验证。动物实验经ICMR-National Institute of Virology (ICMR-NIV), Pune机构动物伦理委员会批准,遵循印度政府CCSEA指南,在ABSL-4最高防护设施中进行;使用3至4月龄亚成年新西兰白兔,通过皮内和皮内-静脉联合途径接种,设置健康对照组。病毒载量通过实时定量PCR(qPCR)检测,利用B6R包膜蛋白基因引物扩增病毒DNA(样本包括拭子、皮肤结痂和组织匀浆)。病毒分离与感染性评估通过在Vero细胞上培养临床样本并观察细胞病变效应(CPE)。抗MPXV兔IgG抗体检测使用γ射线灭活MPXV抗原包被的酶联免疫吸附试验(ELISA)。组织病理学和免疫组织化学评估对福尔马林固定的皮肤和睾丸组织进行苏木精-伊红(H&E)染色,使用抗MPXV小鼠多克隆血清进行免疫组化染色(DAB显色)。 ## 研究结果 **皮内接种与皮内-静脉联合接种的疾病比较:** 在初始实验中,8只兔经皮内途径感染,4只兔经皮内-静脉联合途径感染MPXV Clade IIb.A.2.1变异株。结果显示,皮内组8只中3只出现皮肤病变,而联合组4只全部表现皮肤病变。皮肤病变在感染后约4至5天出现,初为红斑隆起区域,发展为深红色丘疹,于第9至10天形成结痂;痂皮通常在2至3周内脱落,病变在3周末完全消退。病变数量存在个体差异,大小不一。经皮内途径感染的兔中,2/8的口腔和直肠拭子检测到MPXV DNA,3/8的鼻拭子阳性;经联合途径感染的兔中,2只口腔拭子、1只直肠拭子及全部4只鼻拭子均检出MPXV DNA。至感染后第21天,所有感染兔均产生了抗MPXV IgG抗体,皮内组滴度范围为6400至25600,联合组范围为6400至12800。皮肤结痂样本中也检出MPXV DNA。联合接种途径在诱导皮肤病变方面更为有效,因此被选作用Clade Ib和IIb.A.2.1比较研究的感染方式。 **Clade Ib与Clade IIb.A.2.1病毒的疾病比较:** 在第二项实验中,分别用Clade Ib和Clade IIb.A.2.1分离株(各5只雄性兔)经皮内-静脉联合途径感染。两组兔在14天研究期间均表现正常食欲和活动,无明显体重下降。两组在第一周均出现轻微(非显著性)平均直肠温度升高。至感染后第14天,两组均检出抗MPXV IgG抗体,Clade Ib组终点滴度范围为400至1600,Clade IIb.A.2.1组为6400至12800,后者显著高于前者。皮疹约在感染后第5天开始出现,表现为不同大小的红斑隆起区域,部分发展为深红色丘疹并形成结痂。Clade IIb.A.2.1感染兔表现出15至50多个病灶,Clade Ib感染兔为4至25个病灶。在一只Clade IIb.A.2.1感染兔中观察到阴囊皮肤弥漫性病变,并发展为结痂和痂皮形成。 **病毒脱落与器官嗜性:** MPXV DNA仅在感染后第8和10天于两组各2/5兔的口腔拭子中检出;而鼻拭子中的病毒DNA从第4天即可检出并持续至第14天,在第8和10天时所有兔鼻拭子均为阳性。从第8天的鼻拭子样本(PCR循环阈值(Ct)范围21至34)和皮肤结痂样本(Ct范围17至36)中成功分离出病毒。第14天收集的睾丸样本中,Clade Ib组4/5和Clade IIb.A.2.1组4/5检测到病毒DNA,并成功分离出病毒。此外,Clade Ib和IIb.A.2.1组各有1只兔肺样本、Clade Ib组3只兔肝和脾样本检出病毒DNA,但未能分离到病毒;淋巴结样本均为阴性。 **组织病理学和免疫组织化学:** 皮肤样本组织病理学检查显示真皮内大量炎性细胞浸润及表皮结痂形成;结痂由坏死角质形成细胞、中性粒细胞和红细胞组成。在皮内接种部位观察到显著的炎性细胞浸润。免疫组化染色显示皮肤结痂和表皮细胞呈阳性。睾丸组织学检查显示,Clade Ib组4/5和Clade IIb.A.2.1组3/5兔出现不同程度的生精上皮和精母细胞丢失;2只Clade IIb.A.2.1感染兔睾丸组织形态正常。免疫组化在MPXV PCR阳性兔的曲细精管精原细胞和精母细胞中观察到阳性染色。肺和肝脏组织未见组织学改变。 ## 讨论与结论 研究人员在讨论中指出,该兔模型通过皮内-静脉联合接种方式成功模拟了人类Mpox的多种临床特征,包括皮肤丘疹、结痂和血清转换。人类Mpox以发热、皮疹和淋巴结肿大为特征,生殖器周围皮肤是病变主要部位之一;研究人员在部分感染兔中出现皮肤病变并向结痂发展,但未观察到脓疱阶段。MPXV可从兔的鼻拭子、皮肤结痂和睾丸组织中分离到,这与人类感染中皮肤病变和上呼吸道排毒的观察相一致。MPXV抗原在睾丸精母细胞中的定位与该病毒在非人灵长类模型中间质细胞和曲细精管中的抗原检测结果相符。 不同接种途径的比较显示,单独皮内注射组表现出更高的IgG应答,但皮肤病变和病毒脱落的兔数量少于联合接种组,提示皮内途径可能诱导更快的免疫应答,而静脉途径可能使病毒绕过宿主免疫防御,从而利于疾病发展。既往研究曾表明成年兔对MPXV易感性存在差异,年幼兔更易感且常导致致死性疾病,而成年兔易感性相对较低;在成年兔中,既往仅报告静脉接种途径可导致皮疹。在本研究中,亚成年兔(3至4月龄)经皮内途径感染有3/8出现全身性皮肤病变,与早期研究结果不同,这可能与兔的年龄、病毒剂量和毒株差异有关。 值得注意的是,既往在致死性Cast/EiJ小鼠模型中,Clade I病毒较Clade II毒力更强;近期研究在Cast/EiJ小鼠中也报道Clade Ib较Clade IIb毒力增加。然而,在兔模型中,研究人员未发现Clade Ib和Clade IIb.A.2.1变异株在疾病表现或毒力方面存在差异。这一结果提示该模型在区分不同病毒株致病性方面的能力可能需要进一步评估。研究存在一定局限性,如样本量较小、基于单一时间点评估器官病理和病毒嗜性等;未来需要更大样本量研究、多时间点的病毒载量和病理动态评估,以及纳入更多当代分离株来验证该模型的适用性。 综上,研究人员成功建立了一个Mpox疾病兔模型,该模型对MPXV Clade Ib和Clade IIb.A.2.1变异株均表现出易感性。在兔模型中比较两种变异株时,未发现Mpox疾病体征、病毒脱落或器官嗜性方面的显著差异。该模型的建立为评估针对MPXV的医疗对策(包括疫苗和治疗药物)提供了有价值的工具。 # 兔模型用于MPXV Clade Ib与IIb.A.2.1致病性比较研究解读## 研究背景与问题Mpox(猴痘)是由正痘病毒属(Orthopoxvirus)病原体引起的人畜共患疾病,近年来已成为日益重要的全球公共卫生问题。该疾病于1970年在刚果民主共和国首次被发现,此后数十年间主要局限于中非和西非地区,感染主要与接触这些地区感染的啮齿动物有关。然而,2022年Mpox发生全球传播,成为非洲以外报告的最大规模流行,促使世界卫生组织(WHO)于2022年7月23日至2023年5月10日宣布其为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件。与此前通常与疫区旅行史或动物接触相关的感染不同,2022年流行与个体间的性接触相关。2024年,中非和东非报告的Mpox病例再次增加,WHO于2024年8月14日第二次宣布国际关注的突发公共卫生事件。系统发育学上,病毒分为两个不同的分支(clade):Clade I(中非或刚果盆地分支)和Clade II(西非分支)。2022年前报告的Mpox疫情平均病死率(CFR),Clade I为10.6%,Clade II低于4%。2022年病毒全球传播后,猴痘病毒(Monkeypox virus,MPXV)的加速微进化被观察到,导致分支进一步细分为亚分支(Ia, Ib, IIa, IIb)和谱系(A, B, C)。2022至2023年全球Mpox病例激增主要与Clade IIb MPXV相关,而Clade Ib则主要在2024年中非和东非感染激增期间报告。Clade IIb.B.1变异株是2022年流行期间报告的主要谱系,Clade IIb.C.1从2023年年中起成为重要变异株,Clade 主要关键技术方法研究人员使用了以下主要技术方法开展研究:病毒分离与生长动力学分析,在Vero(ATCC? CCL-81?)细胞上进行病毒培养、滴定(按Reed和Muench法计算半数组织培养感染量TCID),并通过全基因组测序对病毒分离株进行序列验证。动物实验经ICMR-National Institute of Virology (ICMR-NIV), Pune机构动物伦理委员会批准,遵循印度政府CCSEA指南,在ABSL-4最高防护设施中进行;使用3至4月龄亚成年新西兰白兔,通过皮内和皮内-静脉联合途径接种,设置健康对照组。病毒载量通过实时定量PCR(qPCR)检测,利用B6R包膜蛋白基因引物扩增病毒DNA(样本包括拭子、皮肤结痂和组织匀浆)。病毒分离与感染性评估通过在Vero细胞上培养临床样本并观察细胞病变效应(CPE)。抗MPXV兔IgG抗体检测使用γ射线灭活MPXV抗原包被的酶联免疫吸附试验(ELISA)。组织病理学和免疫组织化学评估对福尔马林固定的皮肤和睾丸组织进行苏木精-伊红(H&E)染色,使用抗MPXV小鼠多克隆血清进行免疫组化染色(DAB显色)。## 研究结果**皮内接种与皮内-静脉联合接种的疾病比较:** 在初始实验中,8只兔经皮内途径感染,4只兔经皮内-静脉联合途径感染MPXV Clade IIb.A.2.1变异株。结果显示,皮内组8只中3只出现皮肤病变,而联合组4只全部表现皮肤病变。皮肤病变在感染后约4至5天出现,初为红斑隆起区域,发展为深红色丘疹,于第9至10天形成结痂;痂皮通常在2至3周内脱落,病变在3周末完全消退。病变数量存在个体差异,大小不一。经皮内途径感染的兔中,2/8的口腔和直肠拭子检测到MPXV DNA,3/8的鼻拭子阳性;经联合途径感染的兔中,2只口腔拭子、1只直肠拭子及全部4只鼻拭子均检出MPXV DNA。至感染后第21天,所有感染兔均产生了抗MPXV IgG抗体,皮内组滴度范围为6400至25600,联合组范围为6400至12800。皮肤结痂样本中也检出MPXV DNA。联合接种途径在诱导皮肤病变方面更为有效,因此被选作用Clade Ib和IIb.A.2.1比较研究的感染方式。**Clade Ib与Clade IIb.A.2.1病毒的疾病比较:** 在第二项实验中,分别用Clade Ib和Clade IIb.A.2.1分离株(各5只雄性兔)经皮内-静脉联合途径感染。两组兔在14天研究期间均表现正常食欲和活动,无明显体重下降。两组在第一周均出现轻微(非显著性)平均直肠温度升高。至感染后第14天,两组均检出抗MPXV IgG抗体,Clade Ib组终点滴度范围为400至1600,Clade IIb.A.2.1组为6400至12800,后者显著高于前者。皮疹约在感染后第5天开始出现,表现为不同大小的红斑隆起区域,部分发展为深红色丘疹并形成结痂。Clade IIb.A.2.1感染兔表现出15至50多个病灶,Clade Ib感染兔为4至25个病灶。在一只Clade IIb.A.2.1感染兔中观察到阴囊皮肤弥漫性病变,并发展为结痂和痂皮形成。**病毒脱落与器官嗜性:** MPXV DNA仅在感染后第8和10天于两组各2/5兔的口腔拭子中检出;而鼻拭子中的病毒DNA从第4天即可检出并持续至第14天,在第8和10天时所有兔鼻拭子均为阳性。从第8天的鼻拭子样本(PCR循环阈值(Ct)范围21至34)和皮肤结痂样本(Ct范围17至36)中成功分离出病毒。第14天收集的睾丸样本中,Clade Ib组4/5和Clade IIb.A.2.1组4/5检测到病毒DNA,并成功分离出病毒。此外,Clade Ib和IIb.A.2.1组各有1只兔肺样本、Clade Ib组3只兔肝和脾样本检出病毒DNA,但未能分离到病毒;淋巴结样本均为阴性。**组织病理学和免疫组织化学:** 皮肤样本组织病理学检查显示真皮内大量炎性细胞浸润及表皮结痂形成;结痂由坏死角质形成细胞、中性粒细胞和红细胞组成。在皮内接种部位观察到显著的炎性细胞浸润。免疫组化染色显示皮肤结痂和表皮细胞呈阳性。睾丸组织学检查显示,Clade Ib组4/5和Clade IIb.A.2.1组3/5兔出现不同程度的生精上皮和精母细胞丢失;2只Clade IIb.A.2.1感染兔睾丸组织形态正常。免疫组化在MPXV PCR阳性兔的曲细精管精原细胞和精母细胞中观察到阳性染色。肺和肝脏组织未见组织学改变。## 讨论与结论研究人员在讨论中指出,该兔模型通过皮内-静脉联合接种方式成功模拟了人类Mpox的多种临床特征,包括皮肤丘疹、结痂和血清转换。人类Mpox以发热、皮疹和淋巴结肿大为特征,生殖器周围皮肤是病变主要部位之一;研究人员在部分感染兔中出现皮肤病变并向结痂发展,但未观察到脓疱阶段。MPXV可从兔的鼻拭子、皮肤结痂和睾丸组织中分离到,这与人类感染中皮肤病变和上呼吸道排毒的观察相一致。MPXV抗原在睾丸精母细胞中的定位与该病毒在非人灵长类模型中间质细胞和曲细精管中的抗原检测结果相符。不同接种途径的比较显示,单独皮内注射组表现出更高的IgG应答,但皮肤病变和病毒脱落的兔数量少于联合接种组,提示皮内途径可能诱导更快的免疫应答,而静脉途径可能使病毒绕过宿主免疫防御,从而利于疾病发展。既往研究曾表明成年兔对MPXV易感性存在差异,年幼兔更易感且常导致致死性疾病,而成年兔易感性相对较低;在成年兔中,既往仅报告静脉接种途径可导致皮疹。在本研究中,亚成年兔(3至4月龄)经皮内途径感染有3/8出现全身性皮肤病变,与早期研究结果不同,这可能与兔的年龄、病毒剂量和毒株差异有关。值得注意的是,既往在致死性Cast/EiJ小鼠模型中,Clade I病毒较Clade II毒力更强;近期研究在Cast/EiJ小鼠中也报道Clade Ib较Clade IIb毒力增加。然而,在兔模型中,研究人员未发现Clade Ib和Clade IIb.A.2.1变异株在疾病表现或毒力方面存在差异。这一结果提示该模型在区分不同病毒株致病性方面的能力可能需要进一步评估。研究存在一定局限性,如样本量较小、基于单一时间点评估器官病理和病毒嗜性等;未来需要更大样本量研究、多时间点的病毒载量和病理动态评估,以及纳入更多当代分离株来验证该模型的适用性。综上,研究人员成功建立了一个Mpox疾病兔模型,该模型对MPXV Clade Ib和Clade IIb.A.2.1变异株均表现出易感性。在兔模型中比较两种变异株时,未发现Mpox疾病体征、病毒脱落或器官嗜性方面的显著差异。该模型的建立为评估针对MPXV的医疗对策(包括疫苗和治疗药物)提供了有价值的工具。

猴痘症状、传播和治疗:您需要了解的信息 - 周日快报

Mpox symptoms, spread and treatment: What you need to know - Sunday Tribune
新闻简报The article from the Sunday Tribune, published on 2026-09-24, provides general information about mpox, including its symptoms, spread, and treatment. Mpox is a viral infection that can affect anyone, with symptoms such as fever, headache, muscle aches, back pain, tiredness, and swollen lymph nodes, often accompanied by a rash or skin lesions. The infection is mainly spread through close physical contact with an infected person, including skin-to-skin contact, bodily fluids, or contaminated objects. It is not generally as easily spread as common respiratory infections. Most people recover within a few weeks, but some groups, like young children and those with weakened immune systems, are at higher risk of severe illness. The article emphasizes the importance of seeking medical advice if symptoms are suspected and outlines prevention measures such as avoiding close contact, not sharing personal items, and washing hands regularly. Diagnosis typically involves testing material from a skin lesion using a PCR test, and specific antiviral treatments may be used in certain cases. The article does not report any specific outbreak or confirmed cases, but it serves as a general informational guide.

本文于2026-09-24发表,关于猴痘症状、传播和治疗的信息基于一般知识和指导,而非特定事件数据。
地点与对象
猴痘可影响任何人,无论年龄或背景,且不限于特定地区或人群。
事件
该文章提供了关于猴痘的一般信息,包括其症状、传播方式和治疗,但未报告任何具体的暴发、病例或确认事件。
规模、影响与风险
猴痘不像常见的呼吸道感染那样容易传播,但密切接触可能导致传播。它可能引起皮疹、发烧、淋巴结肿大、头痛、肌肉疼痛和疲劳,大多数人几周内即可康复。然而,某些群体,如儿童和免疫系统较弱的人,更容易出现严重疾病。
应对、状态与不确定性
文章概述了一般预防措施,如避免密切接触、不共用个人物品以及定期洗手。还提到通过皮肤病变样本的PCR检测进行实验室确诊,并在某些情况下可能使用特定的抗病毒药物药物。文章未报告任何具体调查或应对状态。

Mpox symptoms, spread and treatment: What you need to know - Sunday Tribune

News Brief
The article from the Sunday Tribune, published on 2026-09-24, provides general information about mpox, including its symptoms, spread, and treatment. Mpox is a viral infection that can affect anyone, with symptoms such as fever, headache, muscle aches, back pain, tiredness, and swollen lymph nodes, often accompanied by a rash or skin lesions. The infection is mainly spread through close physical contact with an infected person, including skin-to-skin contact, bodily fluids, or contaminated objects. Original source text (English)
News Sport Lifestyle Entertainment Opinion Lifestyle Mpox symptoms, spread and treatment: What you need to know Gerry Cupido|Published 1 day ago Ask AI The infection commonly causes a rash or skin lesions, along with symptoms such as fever, headache, muscle aches, back pain, tiredness and swollen lymph nodes. Image: TCHANDROU NITANGA / AFP Mpox is a viral infection that can affect anyone. While it has received increased attention recently, many people are still unsure about how it spreads, what the symptoms look like and when they should seek medical help. The infection is mainly spread through close physical contact with someone who has mpox. It can cause a rash, fever, swollen lymph nodes, headaches, muscle aches and tiredness. Most people recover from mpox within a few weeks, but the infection can cause more serious illness in some people. Here is what you need to know about mpox, including how it spreads, the symptoms to look out for, treatment and how to reduce your risk. What is Mpox? Mpox, previously known as monkeypox, is a viral disease caused by the monkeypox virus, which belongs to the Orthopoxvirus family. The infection commonly causes a rash or skin lesions, along with symptoms such as fever, headache, muscle aches, back pain, tiredness and swollen lymph nodes. Mpox can occur in people of any age or background. There are different groups, or clades, of the virus. Clade I and Clade II have different patterns of circulation, but both can cause mpox in humans. How does Mpox spread? Mpox is mainly spread through close physical contact with an infected person. This can include: Skin-to-skin contact with mpox lesions. Kissing and other close face-to-face contact. Sexual contact. Contact with bodily fluids or lesions. Contact with contaminated clothing, bedding, towels or other objects. Prolonged close contact involving respiratory particles. The virus can also spread from infected animals to people in areas where animal-to-human transmission occurs. Mpox is not generally spread as easily as common respiratory infections such as colds and flu. Close contact is an important route of transmission. What are the symptoms of Mpox? Symptoms can take time to appear after exposure. According to the World Health Organization, they can develop anywhere from one to 21 days after infection. Common symptoms include: Fever Headache Muscle aches Back pain Sore throat Tiredness Swollen lymph nodes A rash or skin lesions For some people, the rash is the first noticeable symptom. The illness often begins with general symptoms before the rash appears, although this is not always the case. Common symptoms include fever and headaches. Image: Diva Plavalaguna / Pexels What does the Mpox rash look like? The rash can appear on different parts of the body, including the face, hands, feet, mouth, groin and genital area. The lesions can change as the infection progresses, developing from flat spots into raised lesions, blisters and pus-filled lesions before crusting over and healing. They can be painful or itchy, and some people develop only a few lesions while others develop many. The rash can last for two to four weeks and may leave scars. Because mpox can resemble other illnesses and skin conditions, a rash should not be diagnosed based on appearance alone. Who is at greater risk of severe Mpox? Most people recover from mpox, but some groups have a higher risk of complications or severe illness. These include: Young children, particularly infants. Pregnant people. People with weakened immune systems. People living with poorly controlled HIV. People with certain other conditions that affect the immune system. Anyone who is concerned about their symptoms or has been exposed to someone with mpox should seek medical advice. How is Mpox diagnosed? Mpox can resemble conditions such as chickenpox, herpes, scabies and some bacterial skin infections, so symptoms alone may not be enough to confirm an infection. A healthcare professional can assess the symptoms and arrange testing where appropriate. Laboratory confirmation is generally done by testing material from a skin lesion, usually using a PCR test. A PCR test is used to determine if it's Mpox. Image: Cottonbro Studio / Pexels How is Mpox treated? There is no routine treatment that simply makes mild mpox disappear immediately. Treatment generally focuses on managing symptoms, looking after the skin, controlling pain and preventing complications or secondary infections. Most people recover without specific treatment within a few weeks. Specific antiviral medicines may be used in certain circumstances, particularly for people with severe illness or those at higher risk of complications. Their availability and use depend on local health systems and clinical guidance. What should you do if you think you have mpox? If you develop an unexplained rash, fever or other symptoms associated with mpox, particularly after close contact with someone who has the infection, contact a healthcare professional. Until you have been assessed, it is important to reduce the risk of passing the virus to other people. You should: Avoid close physical contact with other people. Avoid sharing towels, bedding, clothing and other personal items. Wash your hands regularly with soap and water or use hand sanitiser. Avoid touching or scratching lesions. Follow medical advice about staying away from other people while you are infectious. People with mpox can remain infectious until their lesions have healed and a new layer of skin has formed. Anyone can get Mpox Mpox is not limited to one particular group of people. Anyone who has close contact with an infected person can potentially become infected. Avoiding stigma is therefore important, as fear or embarrassment should not prevent someone from seeking medical care. If you think you may have been exposed to mpox or develop symptoms that could be consistent with the infection, speak to a healthcare professional for advice and testing. 豪登省卫生部门准备应对猴痘,确认三例病例 - EWN

Gauteng Health ready to respond to Mpox, after three confirmed cases - EWN
新闻简报豪登省卫生部门确认了三例猴痘病例,促使该省卫生部门准备应对暴发。国家传染病研究所(NICD)于上周五报告了这些病例,卫生官员们现在正在全省范围内加强监测和应对措施。目前,疫苗抗病毒药物有库存,586剂已存放在区域药店的专用冷冻柜中。这些疫苗需要特殊的冷链储存,当有明确需求时,诊所可以申请获得剂量。该部门已在全省五个卫生区激活了暴发应对团队,由专业团队监测疑似和确诊病例。通过可报告医疗状况的监测系统进行每日病例监测,并且也在利用污水监测作为早期预警机制。居民被敦促保持警惕,识别猴痘的症状和体征,以便尽早寻求医疗帮助。该部门还与国家卫生当局保持沟通,以确保在必要时能够获得额外的疫苗剂量。
查看新闻五要素
时间
这些病例于上周五报告,卫生官员们现在正在全省范围内加强监测和应对措施。发布日期为2026年9月22日。
地点与对象
南非豪登省。卫生部门正关注该省的人口和卫生区。
事件
豪登省卫生部门确认了三例猴痘病例,并准备应对暴发。国家传染病研究所(NICD)报告了这些病例,卫生官员们正在加强监测和应对措施。
规模、影响与风险
已报告三例猴痘确诊病例。疫苗抗病毒药物有库存,目前有586剂存放在区域药店的专用冷冻柜中。卫生官员们通过可报告医疗状况的监测系统每日监测病例,并利用污水监测作为早期预警机制。
应对、状态与不确定性
暴发应对团队已在全省五个卫生区全部启动,专业团队正在监测疑似和确诊病例。该部门正与国家卫生部门沟通,以确保在需要时能够获得额外的疫苗剂量。居民被敦促保持警惕,识别猴痘的症状和体征。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Gauteng Health ready to respond to Mpox, after three confirmed cases - EWN

News Brief
Gauteng Health has confirmed three cases of Mpox, prompting the province's health department to prepare for an outbreak response. The National Institute for Communicable Diseases (NICD) reported the cases last Friday, and health officials are now strengthening surveillance and response measures across the province. Mpox vaccines are available in stock, with 586 doses currently stored in dedicated freezers at regional pharmacies. These vaccines require special cold-chain storage, and clinics can request doses when there is an identified need. The department has activated outbreak response teams in all five health districts, with specialist teams monitoring suspected and confirmed cases. Daily monitoring of cases is conducted through the notifiable medical conditions surveillance system, and wastewater surveillance is also being used as an early warning mechanism. Residents are urged to remain vigilant and recognize the signs and symptoms of Mpox to seek medical care early. The department is also in communication with the national health authorities to ensure additional vaccine doses can be accessed if necessary.
Original source text (English)

Gauteng Health Spokesperson Steve Mabona said, “The department is also engaging with the national department of health to ensure that additional vaccine doses can be accessed should the need arise.” The department is also urging residents to remain vigilant and to recognise the signs and symptoms of Mpox and seek medical care early. Time
The cases were reported last Friday, with health officials now strengthening surveillance and response measures across the province. The publication date is 22 September 2026.
Location and population
Gauteng province, South Africa. The health department is focusing on the province's population and health districts.
Event
Gauteng Health has confirmed three cases of Mpox and is preparing to respond to the outbreak. The National Institute For Communicable Diseases (NICD) reported the cases, and health officials are strengthening surveillance and response measures.
Scale, impact and risk
Three confirmed cases of Mpox have been reported. Mpox vaccines are available in stock with 586 doses currently in dedicated freezers at regional pharmacies. Health officials are monitoring cases daily through the notifiable medical conditions surveillance system and using wastewater surveillance as an early warning mechanism.
Response, status and uncertainty
Outbreak response teams are active across all five health districts, with specialist teams monitoring suspected and confirmed cases. The department is engaging with the national department of health to access additional vaccine doses if needed. Residents are urged to remain vigilant and recognize the signs and symptoms of Mpox.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

NV-387作为治疗用于猴痘(猴痘)的II期临床试验在刚果民主共和国(DRC)启动,NanoViricides宣布

Phase II Clinical Trial of NV-387 as a Treatment for Monkeypox (MPox) Has Begun in DRC, Announces NanoViricides
新闻简报NanoViricides已在刚果民主共和国(DRC)启动了NV-387作为治疗用于猴痘病毒感染的II期临床试验,首批患者于2026年9月23日入组。该试验由Vivi Maketa Tevuzula教授领导,旨在评估该药物在治疗猴痘(特别是Clade I)方面的安全性和有效性,Clade I在DRC为地方性流行。该试验计划招募100名患者,其中已有50名患者完成随机分组,并计划在2026年上半年进行中期疗效分析。DRC的试验地点是一个偏远、资源匮乏的地区,存在持续的猴痘病例,试验的设置需要数月的努力。如果NV-387被证明有效,它可能成为应对疫情的关键药物,因为目前尚无批准用于猴痘的抗病毒药物。此前针对特考韦瑞(TPOXX)的试验未能显示其对Clade I和Clade II 猴痘的有效性,引发了对其在生物恐怖袭击场景中应用的担忧。该试验是应对美国和DRC地区猴痘疫情的更广泛努力的一部分,其中Clade I病例正在增加,而Clade II则在低水平传播,主要在男男性行为者(MSM)中发现。
查看新闻五要素
时间
NV-387的II期临床试验于2026年9月23日在刚果民主共和国(DRC)启动,首批患者入组。该试验计划在2026年上半年再招募50名患者以进行中期疗效分析。
地点与对象
该临床试验在刚果民主共和国(DRC)的Sankuru省Lodja地区进行。试验针对表现出猴痘疾病临床症状且实验室确诊或疑似hmpxv感染的患者。该地区是一个偏远、资源匮乏的地区,存在持续的猴痘病例。
事件
NanoViricides宣布在DRC启动NV-387作为治疗用于猴痘病毒感染的II期临床试验。该试验由Vivi Maketa Tevuzula教授(MD,MSc,PhD)领导,并涉及多个国际中心。试验旨在评估NV-387在治疗猴痘方面的安全性和有效性。
规模、影响与风险
该试验计划招募100名患者,其中已有50名患者完成随机分组。试验重点针对在DRC地方性流行的猴痘 Clade I。目前尚无批准用于猴痘的药物,此前针对特考韦瑞(TPOXX)的试验未能显示其对Clade I和Clade II 猴痘的有效性。如果成功,NV-387可能成为应对疫情的关键药物。
应对、状态与不确定性
该试验正在进行中,首批患者已入组。数据安全监测委员会(DSMB)在前50名患者中未发现新的安全问题。由于试验地点位于资源匮乏的地区,临床试验站点的设置需要数月的努力。该试验是应对美国和DRC地区猴痘疫情的更广泛努力的一部分。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Phase II Clinical Trial of NV-387 as a Treatment for Monkeypox (MPox) Has Begun in DRC, Announces NanoViricides

News Brief
NanoViricides has initiated a Phase II clinical trial of NV-387 as a treatment for mpox virus infection in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC), with the first patients enrolled on September 23, 2026. The trial, led by Professor Vivi Maketa Tevuzula, is designed to evaluate the drug's safety and efficacy in treating mpox, particularly Clade I, which is endemic in the DRC. The trial plans to enroll 100 patients, with 50 already randomized, and aims to perform an interim efficacy analysis in the first half of 2026. The DRC site is a remote, resource-poor region with ongoing mpox cases, and the trial's setup required several months of effort. NV-387 could become a critical pandemic response drug if it proves effective, as there are currently no approved treatments for mpox. Previous trials of tecovirimat (TPOXX) have failed to demonstrate efficacy against both Clade I and Clade II mpox, raising concerns about its utility in bioterrorism scenarios. The trial is part of a broader effort to address mpox in the USA and DRC, where Clade I cases have been increasing, and Clade II is circulating at low levels, primarily among men having sex with men (MSM).
Original source text (English)
Skip to content This page contains press release content distributed by XPR Media. Members of the editorial and news staff of the USA TODAY Network were not involved in the creation of this content. The placement of this content is part of a paid service. Phase II Clinical Trial of NV-387 as a Treatment for Monkeypox (MPox) Has Begun in DRC, Announces NanoViricides NanoViricides SHELTON, CT / ACCESS Newswire / September 23, 2026 / NanoViricides, Inc. (NYSE American:NNVC) (the “Company”) today announced that the Phase II Clinical Trial of the Company’s Novel Broad-Spectrum Antiviral Drug NV-387 as a Treatment for Monkeypox Virus Infection has begun with enrollment of first patients in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC). This clinical trial was registered in the Pan African Clinical Trials Registry database (pactr.samrc.ac.za). The unique identification number for this clinical trial is PACTR202609506749917. The clinical trial is entitled: “An adaptive international, multi-center, randomized, open-label, interventional, parallel group, phase II (IIa and IIb) clinical trial to evaluate the efficacy and safety of a new chemical entity NV-387 formulated as oral gummies in comparison to standard of care, administered in patients with clinical signs of mpox disease and laboratory confirmed or presumptive hmpxv infection.” The Principal Investigator for this clinical trial is Prof. Vivi Maketa Tevuzula, MD, MSc, PhD, Professor in the Department of Tropical Medicine at the University of Kinshasa and Principal Investigator at the Institut Multisectoriel pour l’Amélioration du Bien-être (IMABE), Kinshasa, DRC. Her work focuses on infectious diseases, including clinical trials demonstrating the safety and efficacy of novel drugs, and safety, immunogenicity, and efficacy of vaccines, as well as health systems research in resource-limited settings. She has served as principal investigator on multiple international studies on malaria, Mpox, and vaccine-preventable diseases, and leads interdisciplinary research on genomic surveillance and One Health approaches. Bayer Foundation awarded Dr. Vivi Maketa with the prestigious Early Excellence in Science Award 2023 in Medical Science, recognizing her exceptional contributions to the design and implementation of research projects on infectious and neglected tropical diseases i. “We are pleased to have Professor Maketa lead this clinical trial,” said Anil R. Diwan, PhD, President and Executive Chairman of the Company, adding, “This is the very first Phase II clinical trial of the broad-spectrum antiviral drug NV-387 and aims to explore its safety and effectiveness in Mpox.” There is no approved drug for Mpox. Clinical trials of a drug called tecovirimat (TPOXX, SIGA) have previously failed to demonstrate efficacy against both Mpox Clade II and Mpox Clade I. Tecovirimat was approved by US FDA for Smallpox under the Animal Rule provision. A pan-African clinical trial called “MOSA” of a drug called brincidofovir, which is approved by US FDA for Smallpox under the Animal Rule provision, that started in January, 2025, is currently ongoing. As of January 2026, according to a press release, this study’s Data Safety and Monitoring Board (DSMB) did not identify any new safety concern after the first 50 patients were randomized, and the trial plans to enrol a further 50 patients in the first half of 2026, to perform interim efficacy analysis of brincidofovir ii. Brincidofovir requires constant physician care due to dose-limiting liver and gastro-intestinal toxicity concerns iii. NV-387 could become the “go to” pandemic response drug if it is successful in this Phase II MPox clinical trial, and becomes a FDA-licensed (approved) drug. US Government SNS stockpiling contracts for existing smallpox drugs TPOXX and TEMBEXA have been in several hundreds of millions of dollars, representing an equivalent potential opportunity for NV-387. The Phase II clinical trial for NV-387 as a treatment of mpox is being conducted at Lodja in Sankuru province in DRC. As of now, this province is not an ebola-affected region. Professor Maketa’s team is already on site in DRC. In addition, The team of our CRO from India, Om Sai Clinical Research Pvt. Ltd., is also on site in DRC to start the clinical trial. Lodja is a remote location and mpox cases are continuing to occur in that area. This is a resource-poor region. Setting up the clinical sites required several months of effort because of the resource limitations at the hospital. MPox Clade I is endemic in DRC and all cases in the clinical trial are expected to be of the Clade I virus. The other prominent MPox virus, MPox Clade II is substantially less severe an infection than MPox Clade I. MPox Clade II has become endemic in the USA, circulating at low levels. It primarily affects a limited population of Men-having-Sex-with-Men (MSM), because of transmission during sexual activity. MPox is an “Orphan Disease” in the USA. NanoViricides has applied to the US FDA for Orphan Drug Designation (ODD) of NV-387 for the treatment of MPox. This ODD, assuming it is granted, would enable several benefits including frequent meetings with FDA, waiver of certain FDA fees, certain R&D credits, as well as extension in exclusivity in marketing once approved. These ODD benefits can have a positive economic impact for NanoViricides estimated in the range of tens of millions of dollars. MPox Clade I cases in the USA have been slowly increasing. As of August 27, 2026, since November 2024, there have been more than 50 confirmed cases of Mpox Clade I in the USA, all of which were either travelers to Mpox-manifesting countries or regions, or contacts of such travelers, according to the CDC iv. Community spread of the MPXV Clade I is likely occurring, with 3 cases of MPox Clade I with no travel to Africa, in California in unconnected persons, according to the CDC v. However, the potential for a widespread outbreak remains low. From 2023-2025, about 1,700-2,800 cases of Mpox Clade II were confirmed in the USA, mostly occurring in men-having-sex-with men and associated sexual partners, according to the CDC (ibid #4). Clade II is transmitted via skin abrasions. Thus MPox is becoming important in the USA from the perspective of pandemic preparedness and response. Although there is a vaccine originally developed for smallpox, namely, Jynneos, that is in use to prevent MPox (primarily in clade II contacts), its immune protection was found to wane rapidly in a clinical study vi. The effectiveness of this vaccine is limited, at 36% for one dose and 66% for 2 doses against the less pathogenic MPox Clade II vii. The vaccine effectiveness is likely to be much less against the more severe MPox Clade I. Vaccines do not protect in the first few weeks, limiting their usefulness during pandemic. We believe that there will be a strong opportunity for NV-387 for pandemic preparedness and response for the threats of Mpox and Smallpox in the USA if this Phase II clinical trial of NV-387 for the treatment of Mpox is successful. The two drugs in the USA Strategic National Stockpile (SNS), TPOXX and TEMBEXA, would be unsuitable for pandemic response if MPox Clade I spreads. Although both of these drugs are approved for Smallpox, a bioterrorism agent, under the FDA animal rule, the clinical trial failure of tecovirimat against Mpox which is a much less severe and far less lethal disease compared to Smallpox raises questions about its possible utility in a Smallpox bioterrorism event. In addition, the known toxicity profile and warnings for brincidofovir make it unsuitable for wide-scale deployment in a large outbreak scenario. “NV-387, our broad-spectrum antiviral drug is poised to cause a revolution in treatment of viral diseases, just as antibiotics revolutionized the treatment of bacterial diseases,” said Anil R. Diwan, Ph.D., adding “NV-387 is designed to mimic human cells to trap and destroy the virus. This single drug can target over 90-95% of human pathogenic viruses due to this biomimicry, which is reminiscent of the antibiotic penicillin that targets a large number of human pathogenic bacteria.” NV-387 was found to possess strong antiviral activity against an orthopoxvirus in an animal model that is considered an important model to establish potential effectiveness against MPox and Smallpox viruses, as all of these viruses belong to the same family of orthopoxviruses. In fact, NV-387 effectiveness matched the effectiveness of the small chemical drug tecovirimat in two different models of infection, one was direct skin infection, and the other was a direct lung infection, by the virus. Escape of virus from tecovirimat is known to occur by a single point mutation in a viral protein called VP-37. Vaccines, antibodies, and small chemical drugs such as tecovirimat for MPox/Smallpox, or oseltamivir (Tamiflu®), baloxavir (Xofluza®) for Influenza are readily escaped by viruses simply by introduction of small changes that viruses undergo when they are faced with these challenges in the field. In contrast, escape of virus from NV-387 is highly unlikely because no matter how much the virus changes in the field, it continues to use sulfated proteoglycans such as HSPG as “attachment receptor” in order to cause cell infection. NV-387 mimics the sulfated proteoglycan signature feature that the viruses require. NV-387 is a host-mimetic drug that “looks like a cell” to the virus, displaying numerous ligands that mimic the sulfated proteoglycan, enticing the virus to bind to and become engulfed by the NV-387 dynamic shape-shifting polymeric micelle. Therefore development of NV-387, a broad-spectrum host-mimetic, direct-acting antiviral drug that the viruses cannot escape even as they change constantly, will be revolutionary once the drug undergoes regulatory development for approval for use in humans. New viruses and existing viruses acquiring greater pathology and infectivity are bound to keep appearing in time. To combat such threats, we need to develop broad-spectrum drug arsenal that the viruses cannot escape. Vaccines and antibodies simply will not do, and their limitations have become clearly evident during the COVID-19 pandemic. About NanoViricides NanoViricides, Inc. (the “Company”) (www.nanoviricides.com) is a clinical stage company that is creating special purpose nanomaterials for antiviral therapy. The Company’s novel nanoviricideTM class of drug candidates and the nanoviricideTM technology are based on intellectual property, technology and proprietary know-how of TheraCour Pharma, Inc. The Company has a Memorandum of Understanding with TheraCour for the development of drugs based on these technologies for all antiviral infections. The MoU does not include cancer and similar diseases that may have viral origin but require different kinds of treatments. The Company has obtained broad, exclusive, sub-licensable, field licenses to drugs developed in several licensed fields from TheraCour Pharma, Inc. The Company’s business model is based on licensing technology from TheraCour Pharma Inc. for specific application verticals of specific viruses, as established at its foundation in 2005. Our lead drug candidate is NV-387, a broad-spectrum antiviral drug that we plan to develop as a treatment of RSV, COVID, Long COVID, Influenza, and other respiratory viral infections, as well as MPOX/Smallpox infections. Our other advanced drug candidate is NV-HHV-1 for the treatment of Shingles. The Company cannot project an exact date for filing an IND for any of its drugs because of dependence on a number of external collaborators and consultants. The Company is currently focused on advancing NV-387 into Phase II human clinical trials. NV-CoV-2 (API NV-387) is our nanoviricide drug candidate for COVID-19 that does not encapsulate remdesivir. NV-CoV-2-R is our other drug candidate for COVID-19 that is made up of NV-387 with remdesivir encapsulated within its polymeric micelles. The Company believes that since remdesivir is already US FDA approved, our drug candidate encapsulating remdesivir is likely to be an approvable drug, if safety is comparable. Remdesivir is developed by Gilead. The Company has developed both of its own drug candidates NV-CoV-2 and NV-CoV-2-R independently. The Company is also developing drugs against a number of viral diseases including oral and genital Herpes, viral diseases of the eye including EKC and herpes keratitis, H1N1 swine flu, H5N1 bird flu, seasonal Influenza, HIV, Hepatitis C, Rabies, Dengue fever, and Ebola virus, among others. NanoViricides’ platform technology and programs are based on the TheraCour® nanomedicine technology of TheraCour, which TheraCour licenses from AllExcel. NanoViricides holds a worldwide exclusive perpetual license to this technology for several drugs with specific targeting mechanisms in perpetuity for the treatment of the following human viral diseases: Human Immunodeficiency Virus (HIV/AIDS), Hepatitis B Virus (HBV), Hepatitis C Virus (HCV), Rabies, Herpes Simplex Virus (HSV-1 and HSV-2), Varicella-Zoster Virus (VZV), Influenza and Asian Bird Flu Virus, Dengue viruses, Japanese Encephalitis virus, West Nile Virus, Ebola/Marburg viruses, and certain Coronaviruses. The Company intends to obtain a license for RSV, Poxviruses, and/or Enteroviruses if the initial research is successful. As is customary, the Company must state the risk factor that the path to typical drug development of any pharmaceutical product is extremely lengthy and requires substantial capital. As with any drug development efforts by any company, there can be no assurance at this time that any of the Company’s pharmaceutical candidates would show sufficient effectiveness and safety for human clinical development. Further, there can be no assurance at this time that successful results against coronavirus in our lab will lead to successful clinical trials or a successful pharmaceutical product. 猴痘在南非:关于症状、传播和治疗你需要知道的信息 - sundayindependent.co.za

Mpox in South Africa: What you need to know about symptoms, spread, and treatment - sundayindependent.co.za
新闻简报The article from Sunday Independent, published on 2026-09-23, discusses the rising mpox cases in South Africa, with 22 laboratory-confirmed infections reported across three provinces: Western Cape (18 cases), Gauteng (3 cases), and KwaZulu-Natal (1 case). The affected population is predominantly male, aged 23 to 50, with one female case. Preliminary analysis suggests that Clade Ib mpox virus is involved in the recent outbreaks. Health authorities are conducting contact tracing and enhanced surveillance, particularly in the Western Cape, to monitor the spread. The article highlights that mpox is mainly transmitted through close physical contact, including skin-to-skin contact, contaminated personal items, and respiratory droplets. It also mentions the availability of limited supplies of Tecovirimat and the MVA-BN mpox vaccine as part of the outbreak response. The emphasis is on early detection, avoiding close contact, and maintaining good hygiene to reduce transmission risk. The article does not explicitly state the risk level but underscores the importance of prompt medical attention for those with symptoms or recent exposure.
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章发表于2026-09-23。最新的猴痘病例报告是在4天前,当地情况更新中提到有22例实验室确诊感染。
地点与对象
猴痘病例在南非上升,特别是在三个省份:西开普省(18例)、豪登省(3例)和夸祖鲁-纳塔尔省(1例)。受影响人群主要是23至50岁的男性,有一例女性病例报告。
事件
猴痘,此前称为猴痘,是一种由猴痘病毒引起的病毒性疾病。南非卫生部门报告了22例实验室确认的感染,初步分析表明Clade Ib 猴痘病毒可能与近期的暴发有关。
规模、影响与风险
南非记录了22例实验室确认的猴痘病例。当地情况更新指出,该病毒通过密切的体接触传播,卫生部门正在开展接触者追踪和加强的监测。风险并未明确说明,但文章强调了早期检测和避免密切接触的重要性,以限制传播。
应对、状态与不确定性
南非卫生部门和国家传染病研究所(NICD)正在继续进行接触者追踪和加强的监测,特别是在西开普省。特考韦瑞(一种抗病毒药物 治疗)的使用受到限制,由医疗部门决定。文章还提到MVA-BN 猴痘 疫苗作为暴发应对措施的一部分。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Mpox in South Africa: What you need to know about symptoms, spread, and treatment - sundayindependent.co.za

News Brief
The article from Sunday Independent, published on 2026-09-23, discusses the rising mpox cases in South Africa, with 22 laboratory-confirmed infections reported across three provinces: Western Cape (18 cases), Gauteng (3 cases), and KwaZulu-Natal (1 case). The affected population is predominantly male, aged 23 to 50, with one female case. Preliminary analysis suggests that Clade Ib mpox virus is involved in the recent outbreaks. Health authorities are conducting contact tracing and enhanced surveillance, particularly in the Western Cape, to monitor the spread. The article highlights that mpox is mainly transmitted through close physical contact, including skin-to-skin contact, contaminated personal items, and respiratory droplets. It also mentions the availability of limited supplies of Tecovirimat and the MVA-BN mpox vaccine as part of the outbreak response. The emphasis is on early detection, avoiding close contact, and maintaining good hygiene to reduce transmission risk. The article does not explicitly state the risk level but underscores the importance of prompt medical attention for those with symptoms or recent exposure.
Original source text (English)
News Lifestyle Opinion Sport Lifestyle Mpox in South Africa: What you need to know about symptoms, spread, and treatment Gerry Cupido|Published 4 days ago Ask AI Mpox, previously known as monkeypox, is a viral disease caused by the mpox virus. Image: Freepik Mpox cases are rising in South Africa, with health authorities now reporting 22 laboratory-confirmed infections across three provinces. The latest increase has understandably raised questions about how the virus spreads, what symptoms to look for and what to do if you think you have been exposed. Here is what you need to know about mpox in South Africa, including the latest local situation, symptoms, treatment and ways to reduce your risk. The local context: Why Mpox is in the news South Africa has recorded 22 laboratory-confirmed mpox cases, according to the Department of Health's latest update. The Western Cape accounts for 18 cases, with three in Gauteng and one in KwaZulu-Natal. All but one of the 22 patients are male, with those affected ranging in age from 23 to 50. The latest cases include a 47-year-old Cape Town man who recently travelled to Spain. He had initially been classified as a probable case while South African authorities awaited laboratory results from Spanish health authorities. The other three newly confirmed cases include two imported infections involving a German and a Norwegian citizen. Health authorities and the National Institute for Communicable Diseases (NICD) are continuing contact tracing and enhanced surveillance, particularly in the Western Cape. Preliminary analysis of the recent Western Cape clusters indicates that Clade Ib mpox virus is involved. What exactly is Mpox? Mpox, previously known as monkeypox, is a viral disease caused by the mpox virus, which belongs to the Orthopoxvirus family. It commonly causes a rash or skin lesions, along with symptoms such as fever, headache, muscle aches, back pain, tiredness and swollen lymph nodes. Most people recover from mpox, but some can develop more serious illness. There are different groups, or clades, of the virus. Clade Ib has been involved in recent outbreaks in parts of Africa and has also been detected in other countries. How is Mpox transmitted? Mpox is mainly spread through close physical contact with an infected person. This can include: Skin-to-skin contact with mpox lesions. Kissing or other close face-to-face contact. Sexual contact. Contact with contaminated clothing, bedding or towels. Prolonged close contact, including exposure to respiratory droplets. People can also contract mpox through contact with infected animals in areas where the virus circulates in animal populations. Mpox is not spread as easily through casual contact as common respiratory infections such as colds and flu. Close contact remains the main route of transmission. Spotting the signs: Symptoms and what the rash looks like Symptoms usually appear within 5 to 21 days after exposure, according to the Department of Health. Common symptoms include: Fever Headache Muscle aches Back pain Tiredness Swollen lymph nodes A rash or skin lesions The rash can appear on the face, hands, feet, genitals or other parts of the body. The lesions typically begin as flat red spots before becoming raised bumps, fluid-filled blisters and then pus-filled lesions. They eventually crust over and heal. Not everyone will experience exactly the same symptoms, so an unexplained rash should be assessed by a healthcare professional, particularly if there has been recent close contact with a confirmed or suspected case. Who is more at risk of severe illness? While many people recover without serious complications, some groups have a higher risk of severe mpox. These include: Young children, particularly infants. Pregnant people. People with weakened immune systems. People with advanced or uncontrolled HIV. People with certain serious skin conditions. Diagnosis, treatment and home care Mpox can resemble other illnesses, so a rash cannot always be diagnosed by appearance alone. If mpox is suspected, a healthcare professional can arrange laboratory testing, usually using a sample from a skin lesion. Anyone who develops symptoms after close contact with a confirmed or suspected case should seek medical attention and avoid close contact with others while being assessed. How is Mpox treated? There is no routine cure for mild mpox. Treatment generally focuses on managing symptoms while the body fights the infection. This can include treatment for pain and fever, keeping the skin clean and preventing secondary infections. South Africa's current outbreak response includes a limited supply of Tecovirimat, an antiviral treatment, which health authorities can use as part of their response. Its use is determined by healthcare authorities and is not a routine treatment for everyone with mpox. People who become seriously ill or who have a higher risk of complications may require hospital care. What should you do if you have symptoms? If you develop an unexplained rash, fever or other symptoms associated with mpox, particularly after close contact with someone who has the virus, contact a healthcare professional. Until you have been assessed: Avoid close physical contact with other people. Do not touch, scratch or pop lesions. Do not share towels, bedding, clothing or other personal items. Wash your hands regularly with soap and water or use hand sanitiser. Follow medical advice about staying away from others while you are infectious. Early detection and avoiding further close contact can help limit transmission. Can Mpox be prevented? Reducing close contact with someone who has mpox is one of the most important ways to lower your risk. Good hand hygiene and avoiding contact with an infected person's lesions or contaminated personal items can also help prevent spread. South Africa has also secured limited supplies of MVA-BN mpox vaccine as part of its outbreak response. Health authorities will determine where and how available doses are used, with vaccination forming part of a broader response that also includes surveillance and other prevention measures. If you have been in close contact with someone diagnosed with mpox and subsequently develop symptoms, seek medical attention promptly. Health authorities have also stressed the importance of avoiding stigma around mpox. The virus can affect anyone, and people who suspect they may have been exposed should feel able to seek medical care. IOL Lifestyle Get your news on the go.
